casadi::Function Class Reference

Function object. More...

#include <function.hpp>

Detailed Description

A Function instance is a general multiple-input, multiple-output function where each input and output can be a sparse matrix.
For an introduction to this class, see the CasADi user guide.
Function is a reference counted and immutable class; copying a class instance is very cheap and its behavior (with some exceptions) is not affected by calling its member functions.
Each input and output has a name (name_in, name_out) and a fixed sparsity pattern (sparsity_in, sparsity_out). The same object can be evaluated numerically (DM arguments) or symbolically (SX or MX arguments), differentiated (jacobian, forward, reverse, factory), transformed (map, mapaccum, fold, expand), code-generated (generate), serialized (serialize, save) and inspected (print_dimensions, stats, info).

Instances are created from expressions (the SX and MX constructors), from a C body (jit), by combining other functions (conditional, if_else, map, mapaccum) or by the solver constructors nlpsol, qpsol, rootfinder, integrator, external. Printing shows the signature and the class name, e.g. f:(x,y[2])->(r[2],s) SXFunction.

The exceptions to immutability are options changed with change_option and the cache of derivative functions.

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); y = SX.sym("y", 2)
>>> f = Function("f", [x, y], [x*y, sin(x)], ["x", "y"], ["r", "s"])
>>> f
Function(f:(x,y[2])->(r[2],s) SXFunction)
>>> r, s = f(2, [3, 4])
>>> r, s
(DM([6, 8]), DM(0.909297))
>>> f(x=2, y=[3, 4])
{'r': DM([6, 8]), 's': DM(0.909297)}
>>> z = MX.sym("z")
>>> f(z, [3, 4])
(MX(f(z, [3, 4]){0}), MX(f(z, [3, 4]){1}))


>> x = SX.sym('x'); y = SX.sym('y', 2);
>> f = Function('f', {x, y}, {x*y, sin(x)}, {'x', 'y'}, {'r', 's'});
>> f
f:(x,y[2])->(r[2],s) SXFunction
>> [r, s] = f(2, [3; 4]); disp(r), disp(s)
[6, 8]
0.909297
>> res = f('x', 2, 'y', [3; 4]); disp(res.r), disp(res.s)
[6, 8]
0.909297
>> z = MX.sym('z'); [r, s] = f(z, [3; 4]); disp(r), disp(s)
f(z, [3, 4]){0}
f(z, [3, 4]){1}


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x"); y = ca.SX.sym("y", 2);
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x, y], [x*y, sin(x)], ["x", "y"], ["r", "s"])
Function(f:(x,y[2])->(r[2],s) SXFunction)
julia> r, s = f(2, [3, 4])
([6, 8], 0.909297)
julia> f(x=2, y=[3, 4])
Dict{String, DM} with 2 entries:
  "r" => [6, 8]
  "s" => 0.909297
julia> z = ca.MX.sym("z");
julia> f(z, [3, 4])
(f(z, [3, 4]){0}, f(z, [3, 4]){1})

List of available options
IdTypeDescriptionUsed in
ad_weightOT_DOUBLEWeighting factor for derivative calculation.When there is an option of either using forward or reverse mode directional derivatives, the condition ad_weight*nf<=(1-ad_weight)*na is used where nf and na are estimates of the number of forward/reverse mode directional derivatives needed. By default, ad_weight is calculated automatically, but this can be overridden by setting this option. In particular, 0 means forcing forward mode and 1 forcing reverse mode. Leave unset for (class specific) heuristics.casadi::FunctionInternal
ad_weight_spOT_DOUBLEWeighting factor for sparsity pattern calculation calculation.Overrides default behavior. Set to 0 and 1 to force forward and reverse mode respectively. Cf. option "ad_weight". When set to -1, sparsity is completely ignored and dense matrices are used.casadi::FunctionInternal
always_inlineOT_BOOLForce inlining.casadi::FunctionInternal
cacheOT_DICTPrepopulate the function cache. Default: emptycasadi::FunctionInternal
codegen_optionsOT_DICTOptions to be passed to the code generator of jit, e.g. {"stats": true} to record statistics inside the compiled code. Default: nonecasadi::FunctionInternal
compilerOT_STRINGJust-in-time compiler plugin to be used.casadi::FunctionInternal
custom_jacobianOT_FUNCTIONOverride CasADi's AD. Use together with 'jac_penalty': 0. Note: Highly experimental. Syntax may break often.casadi::FunctionInternal
der_optionsOT_DICTDefault options to be used to populate forward_options, reverse_options, and jacobian_options before those options are merged in.casadi::FunctionInternal
derivative_ofOT_FUNCTIONThe function is a derivative of another function. The type of derivative (directional derivative, Jacobian) is inferred from the function name.casadi::FunctionInternal
dumpOT_BOOLDump function to file upon first evaluation. [false]casadi::FunctionInternal
dump_dirOT_STRINGDirectory to dump inputs/outputs and traces to. Make sure the directory exists [.]casadi::FunctionInternal
dump_formatOT_STRINGChoose file format to dump matrices. See DM.from_file [mtx]casadi::FunctionInternal
dump_inOT_BOOLDump numerical values of inputs to file (readable with DM.from_file) [default: false] A counter is used to generate unique names. The counter may be reset using reset_dump_count.casadi::FunctionInternal
dump_outOT_BOOLDump numerical values of outputs to file (readable with DM.from_file) [default: false] A counter is used to generate unique names. The counter may be reset using reset_dump_count.casadi::FunctionInternal
enable_fdOT_BOOLEnable derivative calculation by finite differencing. [default: false]]casadi::FunctionInternal
enable_forwardOT_BOOLEnable derivative calculation using generated functions for Jacobian-times-vector products - typically using forward mode AD - if available. [default: true]casadi::FunctionInternal
enable_jacobianOT_BOOLEnable derivative calculation using generated functions for Jacobians of all differentiable outputs with respect to all differentiable inputs - if available. [default: true]casadi::FunctionInternal
enable_reverseOT_BOOLEnable derivative calculation using generated functions for transposed Jacobian-times-vector products - typically using reverse mode AD - if available. [default: true]casadi::FunctionInternal
error_on_failOT_BOOLThrow exceptions when function evaluation fails (default true).casadi::ProtoFunction

fd_optionsOT_DICTOptions to be passed to the finite difference instancecasadi::FunctionInternal

gather_statsOT_BOOLDeprecated option (ignored): Statistics are now always collected.casadi::FunctionInternal
inputs_checkOT_BOOLThrow exceptions when the numerical values of the inputs don't make sensecasadi::FunctionInternal
is_diff_inOT_BOOLVECTORIndicate for each input if it should be differentiable.casadi::FunctionInternal
is_diff_outOT_BOOLVECTORIndicate for each output if it should be differentiable.casadi::FunctionInternal
jac_penaltyOT_DOUBLEWhen requested for a number of forward/reverse directions, it may be cheaper to compute first the full jacobian and then multiply with seeds, rather than obtain the requested directions in a straightforward manner. Casadi uses a heuristic to decide which is cheaper. A high value of 'jac_penalty' makes it less likely for the heurstic to chose the full Jacobian strategy. The special value -1 indicates never to use the full Jacobian strategycasadi::FunctionInternal
jacobian_optionsOT_DICTOptions to be passed to a Jacobian constructorcasadi::FunctionInternal
jitOT_BOOLUse just-in-time compiler to speed up the evaluationcasadi::FunctionInternal
jit_cleanupOT_BOOLCleanup up the temporary source file that jit creates. Default: truecasadi::FunctionInternal

jit_optionsOT_DICTOptions to be passed to the jit compiler.casadi::FunctionInternal
jit_serializeOT_STRINGSpecify behaviour when serializing a jitted function: SOURCE|link|embed.casadi::FunctionInternal
jit_temp_suffixOT_BOOLUse a temporary (seemingly random) filename suffix for generated code and libraries. This is desired for thread-safety. This behaviour may defeat caching compiler wrappers. Default: truecasadi::FunctionInternal
max_ioOT_INTAcceptable number of inputs and outputs. Warn if exceeded.casadi::FunctionInternal

SX/SXFunction_constr_oct.m, SX/SXFunction_constr_py.py, integrators/simulator.py, solvers/callback.py, solvers/exacthessian.py, and solvers/ipopt.py.

Constructor & Destructor Documentation

◆ Function() [1/7]

casadi::Function::Function ( )

A null Function has name "null", is_null() is true, and any other member function raises an error. Use it only as a placeholder to be assigned later.

>>> f = Function()
>>> f.is_null(), f.name()
(True, 'null')


>> f = Function(); disp(f.is_null()), disp(f.name())
1
null


julia> f = ca.Function();
julia> ca.is_null(f), ca.name(f)
(true, "null")

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1uy

◆ Function() [2/7]

casadi::Function::Function ( const std::string &  name,
const std::vector< SX > &  ex_in,
const std::vector< SX > &  ex_out,
const Dict &  opts = Dict() 
)

The result is an SXFunction: a flat list of scalar instructions that is cheap to evaluate but has no notion of matrix structure (see expand for the difference with an MXFunction).

Every symbolic primitive that appears in ex_out must be present in ex_in, otherwise construction fails with "Initialization failed since variables [...] are free" (the symbol was forgotten in the input list). Pass option "allow_free": true to construct anyway; such a function can only be called symbolically. Each input must be a purely symbolic matrix (symbols or a concatenation of symbols); an output may be any expression, including constants and empty matrices. The sparsity patterns of the expressions become sparsity_in and sparsity_out.

Parameters
nameDisplay name, used in error messages and generated code. Must start with a letter and contain only letters, digits and non-consecutive underscores (see check_name); otherwise an error is raised.
ex_inList of input expressions, each a symbolic SX
ex_outList of output expressions
name_inNames of the inputs; defaults to i0, i1, ...; duplicates raise an error unless option "allow_duplicate_io_names" is set
name_outNames of the outputs; defaults to o0, o1, ...
optsOptions, see the table below. The common ones are "cse" (eliminate common subexpressions), "live_variables", "is_diff_in"/"is_diff_out", "default_in", "allow_free", "jit"

The dict overload takes a single map from name to expression; name_in and name_out select which keys are inputs and outputs, in that order. A key that is in neither list raises "Unknown dictionary entry". A missing key gives an empty (0-by-0) input or output.

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); y = SX.sym("y", 2)
>>> f = Function("f", [x, y], [x*y, sin(x)])
>>> f
Function(f:(i0,i1[2])->(o0[2],o1) SXFunction)
>>> f = Function("f", [x, y], [x*y, sin(x)], ["x", "y"], ["r", "s"])
>>> f.name_in(), f.name_out()
(['x', 'y'], ['r', 's'])
>>> g = Function("g", {"x": x, "y": y, "r": x*y}, ["x", "y"], ["r"])
>>> g
Function(g:(x,y[2])->(r[2]) SXFunction)
>>> p = SX.sym("p")
>>> try:
...   Function("h", [x], [x*p])
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("Initialization failed" in str(e), "[p] are free" in str(e))
True True
>>> h = Function("h", [x], [x*p], {"allow_free": True})
>>> h.get_free()
['p']
>>> try:
...   Function("my func", [x], [x])
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("Function name is not valid" in str(e))
True


>> x = SX.sym('x'); y = SX.sym('y', 2);
>> f = Function('f', {x, y}, {x*y, sin(x)}); disp(f)
f:(i0,i1[2])->(o0[2],o1) SXFunction
>> f = Function('f', {x, y}, {x*y, sin(x)}, {'x', 'y'}, {'r', 's'});
>> f.name_in(), f.name_out()
x
y
r
s
>> g = Function('g', struct('x', x, 'y', y, 'r', x*y), {'x', 'y'}, {'r'}); disp(g)
g:(x,y[2])->(r[2]) SXFunction
>> p = SX.sym('p');
>> try, Function('h', {x}, {x*p}); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, '[p] are free')); end
1
>> h = Function('h', {x}, {x*p}, struct('allow_free', true)); disp(h.get_free())
p
>> try, Function('my func', {x}, {x}); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'not valid')); end
1


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x"); y = ca.SX.sym("y", 2);
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x, y], [x*y, sin(x)])
Function(f:(i0,i1[2])->(o0[2],o1) SXFunction)
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x, y], [x*y, sin(x)], ["x", "y"], ["r", "s"]);
julia> ca.name_in(f), ca.name_out(f)
(["x", "y"], ["r", "s"])
julia> g = ca.Function("g", Dict("x" => x, "y" => y, "r" => x*y), ["x", "y"], ["r"])
Function(g:(x,y[2])->(r[2]) SXFunction)
julia> p = ca.SX.sym("p");
julia> try
           ca.Function("h", [x], [x*p])
       catch e
           msg = sprint(showerror, e)
           println(occursin("Initialization failed", msg), " ",
                   occursin("[p] are free", msg))
       end
true true
julia> h = ca.Function("h", [x], [x*p], Dict("allow_free" => true));
julia> ca.get_free(h)
1-element Vector{String}:
 "p"
julia> try
           ca.Function("my func", [x], [x])
       catch e
           println(occursin("Function name is not valid", sprint(showerror, e)))
       end
true

See also

>> h = Function('h', {x}, {x*p}, struct('allow_free', true)); disp(h.get_free())
p
>> try, Function('my func', {x}, {x}); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'not valid')); end
1

◆ Function() [5/7]

casadi::Function::Function ( const std::string &  name,
const std::vector< MX > &  ex_in,
const std::vector< MX > &  ex_out,
const Dict &  opts = Dict() 
)

The result is an MXFunction: a graph of matrix-valued nodes that may embed calls to other Function objects (solvers, integrators, SX functions, ...). Compared with an SXFunction it uses less memory and supports every operation, but is slower per node to evaluate; call expand to convert when all nodes are expandable.

The rules on free variables, naming and the dict overload are the same as for the SX constructor. Additional MX-only options: "post_expand" (expand right after construction) and "post_expand_options".

Parameters
nameDisplay name, must satisfy check_name
ex_inList of symbolic MX inputs
ex_outList of MX output expressions
name_inInput names; defaults to i0, i1, ...
name_outOutput names; defaults to o0, o1, ...
optsOptions, see the table below

>>> x = MX.sym("x", 2); A = MX.sym("A", 2, 2)
>>> f = Function("f", [A, x], [A @ x, norm_2(x)], ["A", "x"], ["Ax", "nrm"])
>>> f
Function(f:(A[2x2],x[2])->(Ax[2],nrm) MXFunction)
>>> f.class_name(), f.n_instructions()
('MXFunction', 7)
>>> f.expand()
Function(f:(A[2x2],x[2])->(Ax[2],nrm) SXFunction)
>>> g = Function("g", [x], [x], {"post_expand": True})
>>> g.class_name()
'SXFunction'


>> x = MX.sym('x', 2); A = MX.sym('A', 2, 2);
>> f = Function('f', {A, x}, {A*x, norm_2(x)}, {'A', 'x'}, {'Ax', 'nrm'}); disp(f)

>> f.class_name(), f.n_instructions()

7
>> f.expand()
f:(A[2x2],x[2])->(Ax[2],nrm) SXFunction
>> g = Function('g', {x}, {x}, struct('post_expand', true)); disp(g.class_name())
SXFunction

◆ activity()

std::vector<bool> casadi::Function::activity ( const std::vector< bool > &  arg) const

arg is a flat activity mask over all input nonzeros (size nnz_in(), true = active = possibly nonzero); the result is the corresponding mask over all output nonzeros (size nnz_out()). An output that comes out inactive (false) is guaranteed zero for any value of the active inputs. Unlike sparsity propagation this exploits annihilation: an inactive operand of a multiply kills the product.

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_2ih

◆ bspline()

static Function casadi::Function::bspline ( const std::string &  name,
const std::vector< std::vector< double > > &  knots,
const std::vector< double > &  coeffs,
const std::vector< casadi_int > &  degree,
casadi_int  m = 1,
const Dict &  opts = Dict() 
)
static

The result maps a point of dimension len(knots) (one column vector input) to the m-dimensional spline value. Coefficients are stored flat in coeffs with the value dimension m fastest, then the first spline dimension, etc.; the number of coefficients per dimension is len(knots[d]) - degree[d] - 1. A mismatch between the lengths of knots and degree raises an error. For data-driven splines or general interpolation see interpolant.

Parameters
nameName of the result
knotsKnot vector per dimension, non-decreasing, with repeated end knots
coeffsFlat coefficient list
degreePolynomial degree per dimension
mDimension of the spline value
opts"lookup_mode" (per dimension: "linear", "exact", "binary") and Function options

>>> f = Function.bspline("f", [[0, 0, 0, 1, 2, 2, 2]], [0, 1, 2, 3], [2])
>>> f
Function(f:(i0)->(o0) MXFunction)
>>> f(0), f(0.5), f(2)
(DM(0), DM(0.875), DM(3))
>>> g = Function.bspline("g", [[0, 0, 1, 1], [0, 0, 1, 1]], [0, 1, 2, 3], [1, 1])
>>> g([0.5, 0.5])
DM(1.5)
>>> try:
...   Function.bspline("f", [[0, 0, 0, 1, 2, 2, 2]], [0, 1, 2, 3], [2, 2])
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("must match knot list length" in str(e))
True


>> knots = {[0, 0, 0, 1, 2, 2, 2]};
>> f = Function.bspline('f', knots, [0, 1, 2, 3], 2); disp(f)
f:(i0)->(o0) MXFunction
>> [f(0), f(0.5), f(2)]
[[0, 0.875, 3]]
>> g = Function.bspline('g', {[0, 0, 1, 1], [0, 0, 1, 1]}, [0, 1, 2, 3], [1, 1]);
>> g([0.5; 0.5])
1.5
>> try, Function.bspline('f', knots, [0, 1, 2, 3], [2, 2]); catch e, msg = e.message; end
>> contains(msg, 'must match knot list length')
1

◆ cache()

Dict casadi::Function::cache ( ) const

jacobian, forward, reverse, wrap and serial map store their result in a cache keyed by name (jac_f, fwd1_f, adj1_f, wrap_f, map3_f, ...). The cache holds weak references: an entry stays only while some other reference keeps the function alive. Option "cache" prepopulates it, e.g. with a hand-written Jacobian.

>>> x = SX.sym("x")
>>> f = Function("f", [x], [sin(x)])
>>> f.cache()
{}
>>> J = f.jacobian()
>>> list(f.cache().keys())
['jac_f']


>> x = SX.sym('x');
>> f = Function('f', {x}, {sin(x)});
>> isempty(fieldnames(f.cache()))
1
>> J = f.jacobian(); disp(strjoin(fieldnames(f.cache()), ' '))
jac_f


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x");
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x], [sin(x)]);
julia> ca.cache(f)
Dict{String, Any}()
julia> J = ca.jacobian(f);
julia> collect(keys(ca.cache(f)))
1-element Vector{String}:
 "jac_f"

See also
jacobian, forward, reverse

◆ call() [1/6]

void casadi::Function::call ( const DMDict &  arg,
DMDict &  res,
bool  always_inline = false,
bool  never_inline = false 
) const

This is the general evaluation entry point; the call operator (f(...) in Python, C++ and MATLAB) is a shorthand for it. Argument lists must have exactly n_in() entries. A dict maps input names to values; missing inputs take default_in, unknown names raise an error. The result is a list of n_out() values, or a dict keyed by output name.

The argument type selects the evaluation mode:

  • DM (or anything convertible to DM, such as numbers and numeric arrays): numerical evaluation, returns DM
  • SX: symbolic evaluation producing an SX expression; an MX function is expanded on the fly (fails if it contains non-expandable nodes)
  • MX: symbolic evaluation producing a call node f(args){k} in the MX graph, unless the function is inlined

Shapes: each argument must have the input shape, or be a scalar (broadcast to all entries), a transposed vector, an empty matrix (treated as all zeros), or N-by-(P*M) for an N-by-M input (P evaluations, outputs stacked horizontally; other arguments are repeated). Anything else raises an error that lists these rules. Sparsity is projected: entries outside sparsity_in are ignored. When all inputs are scalar and the arguments are matrices of one common shape, the function is applied elementwise. Numerical evaluation of a function with free variables raises "Cannot evaluate ... since variables [...] are free".

Parameters
argList or dict of arguments, all of the same type
resOutput: list or dict of results
always_inlineMX only: expand the call into the caller's graph instead of creating a call node (SX functions are then inlined as scalar operations); combined with never_inline raises an error
never_inlineSX only: keep the call as an opaque call node inside the SX expression instead of inlining it

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); y = SX.sym("y", 2)
>>> f = Function("f", [x, y], [x*y, sin(x)], ["x", "y"], ["r", "s"])
>>> f.call([2, [3, 4]])
[DM([6, 8]), DM(0.909297)]
>>> f.call({"x": 2})
{'r': DM([0, 0]), 's': DM(0.909297)}
>>> f(2, [3, 4])
(DM([6, 8]), DM(0.909297))
>>> f(x=2, y=[3, 4])
{'r': DM([6, 8]), 's': DM(0.909297)}
>>> f(2, DM([[3, 5], [4, 6]]))
(DM(
[[6, 10],
 [8, 12]]), DM([[0.909297, 0.909297]]))
>>> f(DM(0, 0), 3)
(DM([0, 0]), DM(0))
>>> z = MX.sym("z"); w = MX.sym("w", 2)
>>> f.call([z, w])
[MX(f(z, w){0}), MX(f(z, w){1})]
>>> f.call([z, w], True)
[MX(vertcat((z*w[0]), (z*w[1]))), MX(sin(z))]
>>> a = SX.sym("a")
>>> f.call([a, [1, 2]])
[SX([a, (2.*a)]), SX(sin(a))]
>>> try:
...   f(1)
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("Incorrect number of inputs: Expected 2, got 1" in str(e))
True
>>> try:
...   f(1, [1, 2, 3])
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("Input 1 (y) has mismatching shape. Got 3-by-1" in str(e))
True


>> x = SX.sym('x'); y = SX.sym('y', 2);
>> f = Function('f', {x, y}, {x*y, sin(x)}, {'x', 'y'}, {'r', 's'});
>> res = f.call({2, [3; 4]}); disp(res{1}), disp(res{2})
[6, 8]
0.909297
>> res = f.call(struct('x', 2)); disp(res.r), disp(res.s)
[0, 0]
0.909297
>> [r, s] = f(2, [3; 4]); disp(r), disp(s)
[6, 8]
0.909297
>> res = f('x', 2, 'y', [3; 4]); disp(res.r), disp(res.s)
[6, 8]
0.909297
>> [r, s] = f(2, DM([3, 5; 4, 6])); disp(r), disp(s)
[[6, 10],
 [8, 12]]
[[0.909297, 0.909297]]
>> [r, s] = f(DM(0, 0), 3); disp(r), disp(s)
[0, 0]
0
>> z = MX.sym('z'); w = MX.sym('w', 2);
>> res = f.call({z, w}); disp(res{1}), disp(res{2})
f(z, w){0}
f(z, w){1}
>> res = f.call({z, w}, true); disp(res{1}), disp(res{2})
vertcat((z*w[0]), (z*w[1]))
sin(z)
>> a = SX.sym('a'); res = f.call({a, [1; 2]}); disp(res{1}), disp(res{2})
[a, (2.*a)]
sin(a)
>> try, f(1); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'Expected 2, got 1')); end
1
>> try, f(1, [1; 2; 3]); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'Got 3-by-1')); end
1


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x"); y = ca.SX.sym("y", 2);
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x, y], [x*y, sin(x)], ["x", "y"], ["r", "s"]);
julia> ca.call(f, [2, [3, 4]])
2-element Vector{DM}:
 DM([6, 8])
 DM(0.909297)
julia> ca.call(f, Dict("x" => 2))
Dict{String, DM} with 2 entries:
  "r" => [0, 0]
  "s" => 0.909297
julia> f(2, [3, 4])
([6, 8], 0.909297)
julia> f(x=2, y=[3, 4])
Dict{String, DM} with 2 entries:
  "r" => [6, 8]
  "s" => 0.909297
julia> r, s = f(2, [3 5; 4 6]);
julia> r
DM(
[[6, 10],
 [8, 12]])
julia> s
DM([[0.909297, 0.909297]])
julia> f(ca.DM(0, 0), 3)
([0, 0], 0)
julia> z = ca.MX.sym("z"); w = ca.MX.sym("w", 2);
julia> ca.call(f, [z, w])
2-element Vector{MX}:
 MX(f(z, w){0})
 MX(f(z, w){1})
julia> ca.call(f, [z, w], true)
2-element Vector{MX}:
 MX(vertcat((z*w[0]), (z*w[1])))
 MX(sin(z))
julia> a = ca.SX.sym("a");
julia> ca.call(f, [a, [1, 2]])
2-element Vector{SX}:
 SX([a, (2.*a)])
 SX(sin(a))
julia> try
           f(1)
       catch e
           msg = sprint(showerror, e)
           println(occursin("Incorrect number of inputs: Expected 2, got 1", msg))
       end
true
julia> try
           f(1, [1, 2, 3])
       catch e
           msg = sprint(showerror, e)
           println(occursin("Input 1 (y) has mismatching shape. Got 3-by-1", msg))
       end
true

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1w7

) const

This is the general evaluation entry point; the call operator (f(...) in Python, C++ and MATLAB) is a shorthand for it. Argument lists must have exactly n_in() entries. A dict maps input names to values; missing inputs take default_in, unknown names raise an error. The result is a list of n_out() values, or a dict keyed by output name.

The argument type selects the evaluation mode:

  • DM (or anything convertible to DM, such as numbers and numeric arrays): numerical evaluation, returns DM
  • SX: symbolic evaluation producing an SX expression; an MX function is expanded on the fly (fails if it contains non-expandable nodes)
  • MX: symbolic evaluation producing a call node f(args){k} in the MX graph, unless the function is inlined

Shapes: each argument must have the input shape, or be a scalar (broadcast to all entries), a transposed vector, an empty matrix (treated as all zeros), or N-by-(P*M) for an N-by-M input (P evaluations, outputs stacked horizontally; other arguments are repeated). Anything else raises an error that lists these rules. Sparsity is projected: entries outside sparsity_in are ignored. When all inputs are scalar and the arguments are matrices of one common shape, the function is applied elementwise. Numerical evaluation of a function with free variables raises "Cannot evaluate ... since variables [...] are free".

Parameters
argList or dict of arguments, all of the same type
resOutput: list or dict of results
always_inlineMX only: expand the call into the caller's graph instead of creating a call node (SX functions are then inlined as scalar operations); combined with never_inline raises an error
never_inlineSX only: keep the call as an opaque call node inside the SX expression instead of inlining it

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); y = SX.sym("y", 2)
>>> f = Function("f", [x, y], [x*y, sin(x)], ["x", "y"], ["r", "s"])
>>> f.call([2, [3, 4]])
[DM([6, 8]), DM(0.909297)]
>>> f.call({"x": 2})
{'r': DM([0, 0]), 's': DM(0.909297)}
>>> f(2, [3, 4])
(DM([6, 8]), DM(0.909297))
>>> f(x=2, y=[3, 4])
{'r': DM([6, 8]), 's': DM(0.909297)}
>>> f(2, DM([[3, 5], [4, 6]]))
(DM(
[[6, 10],
 [8, 12]]), DM([[0.909297, 0.909297]]))
>>> f(DM(0, 0), 3)
(DM([0, 0]), DM(0))
>>> z = MX.sym("z"); w = MX.sym("w", 2)
>>> f.call([z, w])
[MX(f(z, w){0}), MX(f(z, w){1})]
>>> f.call([z, w], True)
[MX(vertcat((z*w[0]), (z*w[1]))), MX(sin(z))]
>>> a = SX.sym("a")
>>> f.call([a, [1, 2]])
[SX([a, (2.*a)]), SX(sin(a))]
>>> try:
...   f(1)
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("Incorrect number of inputs: Expected 2, got 1" in str(e))
True
>>> try:
...   f(1, [1, 2, 3])
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("Input 1 (y) has mismatching shape. Got 3-by-1" in str(e))
True


>> x = SX.sym('x'); y = SX.sym('y', 2);
>> f = Function('f', {x, y}, {x*y, sin(x)}, {'x', 'y'}, {'r', 's'});
>> res = f.call({2, [3; 4]}); disp(res{1}), disp(res{2})
[6, 8]
0.909297
>> res = f.call(struct('x', 2)); disp(res.r), disp(res.s)
[0, 0]
0.909297
>> [r, s] = f(2, [3; 4]); disp(r), disp(s)
[6, 8]
0.909297
>> res = f('x', 2, 'y', [3; 4]); disp(res.r), disp(res.s)
[6, 8]
0.909297
>> [r, s] = f(2, DM([3, 5; 4, 6])); disp(r), disp(s)
[[6, 10],
 [8, 12]]
[[0.909297, 0.909297]]
>> [r, s] = f(DM(0, 0), 3); disp(r), disp(s)
[0, 0]
0
>> z = MX.sym('z'); w = MX.sym('w', 2);
>> res = f.call({z, w}); disp(res{1}), disp(res{2})
f(z, w){0}
f(z, w){1}
>> res = f.call({z, w}, true); disp(res{1}), disp(res{2})
vertcat((z*w[0]), (z*w[1]))
sin(z)
>> a = SX.sym('a'); res = f.call({a, [1; 2]}); disp(res{1}), disp(res{2})
[a, (2.*a)]
sin(a)
>> try, f(1); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'Expected 2, got 1')); end
1
>> try, f(1, [1; 2; 3]); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'Got 3-by-1')); end
1

This is the general evaluation entry point; the call operator (f(...) in Python, C++ and MATLAB) is a shorthand for it. Argument lists must have exactly n_in() entries. A dict maps input names to values; missing inputs take default_in, unknown names raise an error. The result is a list of n_out() values, or a dict keyed by output name.

The argument type selects the evaluation mode:

  • DM (or anything convertible to DM, such as numbers and numeric arrays): numerical evaluation, returns DM
  • SX: symbolic evaluation producing an SX expression; an MX function is expanded on the fly (fails if it contains non-expandable nodes)
  • MX: symbolic evaluation producing a call node f(args){k} in the MX graph, unless the function is inlined

Anything else raises an error that lists these rules. Sparsity is projected: entries outside sparsity_in are ignored. When all inputs are scalar and the arguments are matrices of one common shape, the function is applied elementwise. f(z, w){0}

>> res = f.call({z, w}, true); disp(res{1}), disp(res{2})

>> a = SX.sym('a'); res = f.call({a, [1; 2]}); disp(res{1}), disp(res{2})
[a, (2.*a)]
sin(a)
>> try, f(1); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'Expected 2, got 1')); end
1
>> try, f(1, [1; 2; 3]); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'Got 3-by-1')); end
1

◆ call() [6/6]

void casadi::Function::call ( const SXDict &  arg,
SXDict &  res,
bool  always_inline = false,
bool  never_inline = false 
) const

This is the general evaluation entry point; the call operator (f(...) in Python, C++ and MATLAB) is a shorthand for it. Argument lists must have exactly n_in() entries. A dict maps input names to values; missing inputs take default_in, unknown names raise an error. The result is a list of n_out() values, or a dict keyed by output name.

The argument type selects the evaluation mode:

  • DM (or anything convertible to DM, such as numbers and numeric arrays): numerical evaluation, returns DM
  • SX: symbolic evaluation producing an SX expression; an MX function is expanded on the fly (fails if it contains non-expandable nodes)
  • MX: symbolic evaluation producing a call node f(args){k} in the MX graph, unless the function is inlined

Shapes: each argument must have the input shape, or be a scalar (broadcast to all entries), a transposed vector, an empty matrix (treated as all zeros), or N-by-(P*M) for an N-by-M input (P evaluations, outputs stacked horizontally; other arguments are repeated). Anything else raises an error that lists these rules. Sparsity is projected: entries outside sparsity_in are ignored. When all inputs are scalar and the arguments are matrices of one common shape, the function is applied elementwise. Numerical evaluation of a function with free variables raises "Cannot evaluate ... since variables [...] are free".

Parameters
argList or dict of arguments, all of the same type
resOutput: list or dict of results
always_inlineMX only: expand the call into the caller's graph instead of creating a call node (SX functions are then inlined as scalar operations); combined with never_inline raises an error
never_inlineSX only: keep the call as an opaque call node inside the SX expression instead of inlining it

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); y = SX.sym("y", 2)
>>> f = Function("f", [x, y], [x*y, sin(x)], ["x", "y"], ["r", "s"])
>>> f.call([2, [3, 4]])
[DM([6, 8]), DM(0.909297)]
>>> f.call({"x": 2})
{'r': DM([0, 0]), 's': DM(0.909297)}
>>> f(2, [3, 4])
(DM([6, 8]), DM(0.909297))
>>> f(x=2, y=[3, 4])
{'r': DM([6, 8]), 's': DM(0.909297)}
>>> f(2, DM([[3, 5], [4, 6]]))
(DM(
[[6, 10],
 [8, 12]]), DM([[0.909297, 0.909297]]))
>>> f(DM(0, 0), 3)
(DM([0, 0]), DM(0))
>>> z = MX.sym("z"); w = MX.sym("w", 2)
>>> f.call([z, w])
[MX(f(z, w){0}), MX(f(z, w){1})]
>>> f.call([z, w], True)
[MX(vertcat((z*w[0]), (z*w[1]))), MX(sin(z))]
>>> a = SX.sym("a")
>>> f.call([a, [1, 2]])
[SX([a, (2.*a)]), SX(sin(a))]
>>> try:
...   f(1)
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("Incorrect number of inputs: Expected 2, got 1" in str(e))
True
>>> try:
...   f(1, [1, 2, 3])
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("Input 1 (y) has mismatching shape. Got 3-by-1" in str(e))
True


>> x = SX.sym('x'); y = SX.sym('y', 2);
>> f = Function('f', {x, y}, {x*y, sin(x)}, {'x', 'y'}, {'r', 's'});
>> res = f.call({2, [3; 4]}); disp(res{1}), disp(res{2})
[6, 8]
0.909297
>> res = f.call(struct('x', 2)); disp(res.r), disp(res.s)
[0, 0]
0.909297
>> [r, s] = f(2, [3; 4]); disp(r), disp(s)
[6, 8]
0.909297
>> res = f('x', 2, 'y', [3; 4]); disp(res.r), disp(res.s)
[6, 8]
0.909297
>> [r, s] = f(2, DM([3, 5; 4, 6])); disp(r), disp(s)
[[6, 10],
 [8, 12]]
[[0.909297, 0.909297]]
>> [r, s] = f(DM(0, 0), 3); disp(r), disp(s)
[0, 0]
0
>> z = MX.sym('z'); w = MX.sym('w', 2);
>> res = f.call({z, w}); disp(res{1}), disp(res{2})
f(z, w){0}
f(z, w){1}
>> res = f.call({z, w}, true); disp(res{1}), disp(res{2})
vertcat((z*w[0]), (z*w[1]))
sin(z)
>> a = SX.sym('a'); res = f.call({a, [1; 2]}); disp(res{1}), disp(res{2})
[a, (2.*a)]
sin(a)
>> try, f(1); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'Expected 2, got 1')); end
1
>> try, f(1, [1; 2; 3]); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'Got 3-by-1')); end
1

◆ change_option()

void casadi::Function::change_option ( const std::string &  option_name,
const GenericType &  option_value 
)

Only options that do not change the numerical result can be changed: "verbose", "regularity_check", "print_in", "print_out", "dump", "dump_in", "dump_out", "dump_trace", "dump_dir", "dump_format", "print_time", "record_time" and "print_instructions". Any other existing option raises "cannot be changed"; an unknown name raises "does not exist".

>>> f = Function("f", [SX.sym("x")], [SX(0)])
>>> f.change_option("verbose", False)
>>> try:
...   f.change_option("cse", True)
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("Option 'cse' cannot be changed" in str(e))
True


>> f = Function('f', {SX.sym('x')}, {SX(0)});
>> f.change_option('verbose', false)
>> try, f.change_option('cse', true); catch e, msg = e.message; end
>> contains(msg, 'Option ''cse'' cannot be changed')
1

◆ check_name()

static bool casadi::Function::check_name ( const std::string &  name)
static

A valid name starts with a letter and continues with letters, digits and underscores, with no two consecutive underscores; it may not be one of the keywords "null", "jac", "hess". Constructors and generate raise for invalid names; names must be valid C identifiers because they appear in generated code.

>>> Function.check_name("f_1"), Function.check_name("1f")
(True, False)
>>> Function.check_name("a__b"), Function.check_name("jac"), Function.check_name("")
(False, False, False)


>> fprintf('%d %d\n', Function.check_name('f_1'), Function.check_name('1f'))
1 0
>> fprintf('%d %d\n', Function.check_name('a__b'), Function.check_name('jac'))
0 0
>> Function.check_name('')
0


julia> F = ca.Function;
julia> F.check_name("f_1"), F.check_name("1f")
(true, false)
julia> F.check_name("a__b"), F.check_name("jac"), F.check_name("")
(false, false, false)

◆ checkout()

casadi_int casadi::Function::checkout ( ) const

Each memory object holds the state of one evaluation context (solver workspace, statistics); ordinary calls use index 0. Reserving separate objects allows concurrent evaluation from several threads with the low-level interface and separate stats. Release it with release when done.

>>> f = Function("f", [SX.sym("x")], [SX(0)])
>>> m = f.checkout()
>>> m
1
>>> f.release(m)


>> f = Function('f', {SX.sym('x')}, {SX(0)});
>> m = f.checkout(); disp(m)
1
>> f.release(m)


julia> f = ca.Function("f", [ca.SX.sym("x")], [ca.SX(0)]);
julia> m = ca.checkout(f)
1
julia> ca.release(f, m)

See also
release, stats, FunctionBuffer

◆ conditional() [1/2]

static Function casadi::Function::conditional ( const std::string &  name,
const Function &  f,
const Dict &  opts = Dict() 
)
static

returns zeros otherwise

Shorthand for if_else with a zero-returning function of the same signature as the false branch; evaluating with a zero condition skips f entirely.

>>> x = SX.sym("x")
>>> f = Function("f", [x], [x**2])
>>> c = Function.conditional("c", f)
>>> c, c(0, 3), c(1, 3)
(Function(c:(i0,i1)->(o0) Switch), DM(0), DM(9))


>> x = SX.sym('x');
>> f = Function('f', {x}, {x^2});
>> c = Function.conditional('c', f); disp(c)
c:(i0,i1)->(o0) Switch
>> [c(0, 3), c(1, 3)]
[[0, 9]]


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x");
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x], [x^2]);
julia> c = ca.Function.conditional("c", f);
julia> println(c); c(0, 3), c(1, 3)
c:(i0,i1)->(o0) Switch
(0, 9)

◆ conditional() [2/2]

static Function casadi::Function::conditional ( const std::string &  name,
const std::vector< Function > &  f,
const Function &  f_def,
const Dict &  opts = Dict() 
)
static

The result takes an index as first input followed by the inputs of the branch functions. At evaluation the index is truncated to an integer k; branch f[k] is evaluated when 0 <= k < len(f), otherwise f_def. All branches must have the same number of inputs and outputs; the signature (names, shapes) is taken from f_def and arguments are projected when a branch has a different sparsity. Derivatives are the derivatives of the selected branch. Symbolic MX evaluation gives a call node; unlike if_else on expressions, only the selected branch is evaluated numerically.

Parameters
nameName of the result
fBranch functions, selected by index 0, 1, ...
f_defDefault branch for indices out of range
optsFunction options

>>> x = SX.sym("x")
>>> f0 = Function("f0", [x], [x]); f1 = Function("f1", [x], [x**2])
>>> fdef = Function("fdef", [x], [-x])
>>> sw = Function.conditional("sw", [f0, f1], fdef)
>>> sw
Function(sw:(i0,i1)->(o0) Switch)
>>> sw(0, 3), sw(1, 3), sw(1.9, 3), sw(2, 3), sw(-1, 3)
(DM(3), DM(9), DM(9), DM(-3), DM(-3))
>>> sw(MX.sym("k"), MX.sym("v"))
MX(sw(k, v){0})


>> x = SX.sym('x');
>> f0 = Function('f0', {x}, {x}); f1 = Function('f1', {x}, {x^2});
>> fdef = Function('fdef', {x}, {-x});
>> sw = Function.conditional('sw', {f0, f1}, fdef); disp(sw)
sw:(i0,i1)->(o0) Switch
>> [sw(0, 3), sw(1, 3), sw(1.9, 3), sw(2, 3), sw(-1, 3)]
[[3, 9, 9, -3, -3]]
>> sw(MX.sym('k'), MX.sym('v'))
sw(k, v){0}


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x");
julia> f0 = ca.Function("f0", [x], [x]); f1 = ca.Function("f1", [x], [x^2]);
julia> fdef = ca.Function("fdef", [x], [-x]);
julia> sw = ca.Function.conditional("sw", [f0, f1], fdef);
julia> sw
Function(sw:(i0,i1)->(o0) Switch)
julia> sw(0, 3), sw(1, 3), sw(1.9, 3), sw(2, 3), sw(-1, 3)
(3, 9, 9, -3, -3)
julia> sw(ca.MX.sym("k"), ca.MX.sym("v"))
MX(sw(k, v){0})

See also
if_else

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1wm

◆ convert_in() [1/6]

std::vector<DM> casadi::Function::convert_in ( const DMDict &  arg) const

convert_in turns a list of n_in() values into a dict keyed by input name, and a dict into a list where absent inputs get default_in. convert_out does the same for outputs; absent outputs become nan. An unknown key or a list of the wrong length raises an error. Values are passed through unchanged (no shape or sparsity check), for any of DM, SX and MX. Useful to prepare arguments for call or generate_in.

>>> f = Function("f", [SX.sym("x"), SX.sym("y")], [SX(0), SX(1)], ["x", "y"], ["a", "b"])
>>> f.convert_in([1, 2])
{'x': DM(1), 'y': DM(2)}
>>> f.convert_in({"y": 2})
[DM(0), DM(2)]
>>> f.convert_out({"b": 5})
[DM(nan), DM(5)]
>>> try:
...   f.convert_in({"z": 1})
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print('could not find entry "z"' in str(e))
True


>> f = Function('f', {SX.sym('x'), SX.sym('y')}, {SX(0), SX(1)}, {'x', 'y'}, {'a', 'b'});
>> s = f.convert_in({1, 2}); disp(s.x), disp(s.y)
1
2
>> c = f.convert_in(struct('y', 2)); disp(c{1}), disp(c{2})
0
2
>> c = f.convert_out(struct('b', 5)); disp(c{1}), disp(c{2})
nan
5
>> try, f.convert_in(struct('z', 1)); catch e, msg = e.message; end
>> contains(msg, 'could not find entry "z"')
1


julia> f = ca.Function("f", [ca.SX.sym("x"), ca.SX.sym("y")], [ca.SX(0), ca.SX(1)],
                       ["x", "y"], ["a", "b"]);
julia> ca.convert_in(f, Dict("y" => 2))
2-element Vector{DM}:
 DM(0)
 DM(2)
julia> ca.convert_out(f, Dict("b" => 5))
2-element Vector{DM}:
 DM(nan)
 DM(5)
julia> try
           ca.convert_in(f, Dict("z" => 1))
       catch e
           println(occursin("could not find entry \"z\"", sprint(showerror, e)))
       end
true

See also
call, default_in, nz_from_in

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1x7

◆ default_in()

double casadi::Function::default_in ( casadi_int  ind) const

When calling with a dict (or keyword arguments), every input that is not given is filled with this scalar, broadcast to the input shape. For functions built from expressions it is 0 unless option "default_in" was set at construction. Solvers define their own: nlpsol uses -inf for lbx and lbg, inf for ubx and ubg, 0 for the rest.

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); y = SX.sym("y", 2)
>>> f = Function("f", [x, y], [x + y], ["x", "y"], ["r"])
>>> f.default_in(0), f(x=3)
(0.0, {'r': DM([3, 3])})
>>> g = Function("g", [x, y], [x + y], ["x", "y"], ["r"], {"default_in": [1, 2]})
>>> g.default_in(1), g(x=3), g(y=[0, 1])
(2.0, {'r': DM([5, 5])}, {'r': DM([1, 2])})


>> x = SX.sym('x'); y = SX.sym('y', 2);
>> f = Function('f', {x, y}, {x + y}, {'x', 'y'}, {'r'});
>> disp(f.default_in(0)), res = f('x', 3); disp(res.r)
0
[3, 3]
>> opts = struct('default_in', [1, 2]);
>> g = Function('g', {x, y}, {x + y}, {'x', 'y'}, {'r'}, opts);
>> disp(g.default_in(1)), res = g('x', 3); disp(res.r), res = g('y', [0; 1]); disp(res.r)
2
[5, 5]
[1, 2]


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x"); y = ca.SX.sym("y", 2);
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x, y], [x + y], ["x", "y"], ["r"]);
julia> ca.default_in(f, 0), f(x=3)["r"]
(0.0, [3, 3])
julia> opts = Dict("default_in" => [1, 2]);
julia> g = ca.Function("g", [x, y], [x + y], ["x", "y"], ["r"], opts);
julia> ca.default_in(g, 1), g(x=3)["r"], g(y=[0, 1])["r"]
(2.0, [5, 5], [1, 2])

See also
max_in, min_in, nominal_in, convert_in

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1vm

◆ deserialize() [1/3]

static Function casadi::Function::deserialize ( const std::string &  s)
static

Raises "DeserializingStream sanity check failed" for data that is not a serialized CasADi object.

>>> x = SX.sym("x")
>>> f = Function.deserialize(Function("f", [x], [2*x]).serialize())
>>> f(3)
DM(6)
>>> try:
...   Function.deserialize("not a function")
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("sanity check failed" in str(e))
True


>> x = SX.sym('x');
>> f = Function.deserialize(Function('f', {x}, {2*x}).serialize());
>> f(3)
6
>> try, Function.deserialize('not a function'); catch e, msg = e.message; end
>> contains(msg, 'sanity check failed')
1


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x");
julia> f = ca.Function.deserialize(ca.serialize(ca.Function("f", [x], [2x])));
julia> f(3)
DM(6)
julia> try
           ca.Function.deserialize("not a function")
       catch e
           println(occursin("sanity check failed", sprint(showerror, e)))
       end
true

See also
serialize, load

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1y0

◆ expand() [1/2]

Function casadi::Function::expand ( ) const

Evaluates the function symbolically with SX inputs and builds an SXFunction from the result: every matrix node is unrolled into scalar instructions. Evaluation of the result is typically faster per operation, at the cost of memory proportional to the number of scalar operations. The name, input/output names and the options of the original function (other than the SX-specific ones given in opts) are kept.

Fails when the graph contains a node that has no scalar expansion, with "eval_sx not defined for ...": for example calls to external functions or linear solves (solve, inv) with a numerical plugin; use the "symbolicqr" plugin for those. Fails when the function has free variables. An SXFunction is returned unchanged (as a copy).

Parameters
nameName of the new function; defaults to the current name
optsOptions for the SXFunction constructor, e.g. "live_variables", "cse"
Returns
SXFunction

>>> x = MX.sym("x", 2)
>>> f = Function("f", [x], [x.T @ x, 2*x], ["x"], ["q", "y"])
>>> f, f.n_instructions()
(Function(f:(x[2])->(q,y[2]) MXFunction), 7)
>>> g = f.expand()
>>> g, g.n_instructions()
(Function(f:(x[2])->(q,y[2]) SXFunction), 10)
>>> f([1, 2]), g([1, 2])
((DM(5), DM([2, 4])), (DM(5), DM([2, 4])))
>>> f.expand("f_sx", {"live_variables": False})
Function(f_sx:(x[2])->(q,y[2]) SXFunction)
>>> A = MX.sym("A", 2, 2)
>>> h = Function("h", [A, x], [solve(A, x)])
>>> try:
...   h.expand()
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("eval_sx not defined" in str(e))
True
>>> Function("h", [A, x], [solve(A, x, "symbolicqr")]).expand().class_name()
'SXFunction'


>> x = MX.sym('x', 2);
>> f = Function('f', {x}, {x'*x, 2*x}, {'x'}, {'q', 'y'});
>> f, f.n_instructions()
f:(x[2])->(q,y[2]) MXFunction
7
>> g = f.expand(); disp(g), disp(g.n_instructions())
f:(x[2])->(q,y[2]) SXFunction
10
>> [q, y] = g([1; 2]); disp(q), disp(y)
5
[2, 4]
>> f.expand('f_sx', struct('live_variables', false))
f_sx:(x[2])->(q,y[2]) SXFunction
>> A = MX.sym('A', 2, 2); h = Function('h', {A, x}, {solve(A, x)});
>> try, h.expand(); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'eval_sx not defined')); end
1
>> Function('h', {A, x}, {solve(A, x, 'symbolicqr')}).expand().class_name()
SXFunction


julia> x = ca.MX.sym("x", 2);
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x], [x' * x, 2x], ["x"], ["q", "y"]);
julia> println(f); ca.n_instructions(f)
f:(x[2])->(q,y[2]) MXFunction
7
julia> g = ca.expand(f);
julia> println(g); ca.n_instructions(g)
f:(x[2])->(q,y[2]) SXFunction
10
julia> f([1, 2]), g([1, 2])
((5, [2, 4]), (5, [2, 4]))
julia> ca.expand(f, "f_sx", Dict("live_variables" => false))
Function(f_sx:(x[2])->(q,y[2]) SXFunction)
julia> A = ca.MX.sym("A", 2, 2);
julia> h = ca.Function("h", [A, x], [A \ x]);
julia> try
           ca.expand(h)
       catch e
           println(occursin("eval_sx not defined", sprint(showerror, e)))
       end
true
julia> ca.class_name(ca.expand(ca.Function("h", [A, x], [ca.solve(A, x, "symbolicqr")])))
"SXFunction"

◆ export_code() [1/2]

std::string casadi::Function::export_code ( const std::string &  lang,
const Dict &  options = Dict() 
) const

Only "matlab" is supported, and only for SXFunction and MXFunction without free variables; other classes or languages raise an error. The code is a plain MATLAB function with the same name that evaluates the algorithm on numeric arguments, without any CasADi dependency. The file overload writes to fname, the other returns the code as a string.

Parameters
lang"matlab"
options"indent_level" (integer)

>>> x = SX.sym("x")
>>> f = Function("f", [x], [sin(x)])
>>> f.export_code("matlab").splitlines()[0]
'function [varargout] = f(varargin)'
>>> try:
...   f.export_code("python")
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("Only matlab language supported" in str(e))
True

>> try, f.export_code('python'); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'Only matlab')); end
1

HTML and .casadi_viz embed the serialized Function, decoded by the browser viewer; any serializable Function works, and helpers such as nlp_hess_l can be explored. include_functions (default true) shows called and helper Functions in the viewer. DOT is standalone Graphviz of an SX/MX root graph only. Options show_matrix_contents and show_matrix_sizes (both default true) control matrix detail and dimensions. Load dump_trace JSONL files in the viewer to replay numerical evaluation. Options: view (function, expression; default function), direction (LR, RL, TB, BT; expression view only; default TB), viz_js (local Viz.js global bundle to embed; by default uses the viewer package renderer). viewer_url (string) overrides the HTML viewer ESM module URL; default is https://unpkg.com/@casadi/casadi-viz@MAJOR.MINOR/dist/index.js for this CasADi version.

◆ factory()

Function casadi::Function::factory ( const std::string &  name,
const std::vector< std::string > &  s_in,
const std::vector< std::string > &  s_out,
const AuxOut &  aux = AuxOut(),
const Dict &  opts = Dict() 
) const

Only defined for SXFunction and MXFunction (other classes raise an error). The new function takes the inputs listed in s_in and returns the outputs listed in s_out, in that order. Each entry is either an existing name or a derivative request built from existing names with ':' separators:

  • inputs: "fwd:x" a forward seed for input x, "adj:r" an adjoint seed for output r, "lam:r" a multiplier for output r (used by aux)
  • outputs: "fwd:r" forward sensitivity, "adj:x" adjoint sensitivity, "jac:r:x" the Jacobian of r with respect to x, "grad:r:x" its gradient (r must be scalar), "hess:r:x:y" the Hessian of scalar r with respect to x and y
  • an output may be prefixed with "triu:", "tril:" or "sym:" to return only the upper or lower triangle of a Hessian, or to symmetrize it

The output names of the result have ':' replaced by '_' (jac:r:x becomes jac_r_x). Non-differentiable inputs and outputs (is_diff_in/is_diff_out) give all-zero blocks. An input that is needed by an output but not listed in s_in is treated as zero. An unknown name raises an error listing the available names.

Parameters
nameName of the new function
s_inInput requests, see above
s_outOutput requests, see above
auxExtra scalar outputs defined as linear combinations: {"gamma": ["f", "g"]} defines gamma = lam_f'*f + lam_g'*g, so that "grad:gamma:x" and "hess:gamma:x:x" are the Lagrangian gradient and Hessian with inputs "lam:f" and "lam:g"
opts"helper_options" and "final_options" (dicts for the intermediate and the returned function) and any Function option for the result

>>> x = SX.sym("x", 2); p = SX.sym("p")
>>> f = Function("f", [x, p], [p*dot(x, x), x[0]*x[1]], ["x", "p"], ["f", "g"])
>>> g = f.factory("g", ["x", "p"], ["jac:f:x", "hess:f:x:x", "jac:g:x"])
>>> g
Function(g:(x[2],p)->(jac_f_x[1x2],hess_f_x_x[2x2,2nz],jac_g_x[1x2]) SXFunction)
>>> g([1, 2], 3)
(DM([[6, 12]]), DM(
[[6, 00],
 [00, 6]]), DM([[2, 1]]))
>>> L = f.factory("L", ["x", "p", "lam:f", "lam:g"], ["grad:gamma:x"],
...               {"gamma": ["f", "g"]})
>>> L, L([1, 2], 3, 1, 2)
(Function(L:(x[2],p,lam_f,lam_g)->(grad_gamma_x[2]) SXFunction), DM([10, 14]))
>>> h = f.factory("h", ["x", "p", "fwd:x", "fwd:p"], ["fwd:f"])
>>> h([1, 2], 3, [1, 0], 0)
DM(6)
>>> try:
...   f.factory("bad", ["x"], ["jac:q:x"])
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print('Cannot process "q" as output. Available: f,g.' in str(e))
True


>> x = SX.sym('x', 2); p = SX.sym('p');
>> f = Function('f', {x, p}, {p*dot(x, x), x(1)*x(2)}, {'x', 'p'}, {'f', 'g'});
>> g = f.factory('g', {'x', 'p'}, {'jac:f:x', 'hess:f:x:x', 'jac:g:x'}); disp(g)
g:(x[2],p)->(jac_f_x[1x2],hess_f_x_x[2x2,2nz],jac_g_x[1x2]) SXFunction
>> [jac_f_x, hess_f_x_x, jac_g_x] = g([1; 2], 3);
>> jac_f_x, hess_f_x_x, jac_g_x
[[6, 12]]
[[6, 00],
 [00, 6]]
[[2, 1]]
>> aux = struct('gamma', {{'f', 'g'}});
>> L = f.factory('L', {'x', 'p', 'lam:f', 'lam:g'}, {'grad:gamma:x'}, aux);
>> L, L([1; 2], 3, 1, 2)
L:(x[2],p,lam_f,lam_g)->(grad_gamma_x[2]) SXFunction
[10, 14]
>> h = f.factory('h', {'x', 'p', 'fwd:x', 'fwd:p'}, {'fwd:f'});
>> h([1; 2], 3, [1; 0], 0)
6
>> try, f.factory('bad', {'x'}, {'jac:q:x'}); catch e, msg = e.message; end
>> contains(msg, 'Cannot process "q" as output. Available: f,g.')
1


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x", 2); p = ca.SX.sym("p");

                       ["x", "p"], ["f", "g"]);

◆ find_functions()

std::vector<Function> casadi::Function::find_functions ( casadi_int  max_depth = -1) const

Walks the owned functions (get_function) and their owned functions in turn, returning each distinct Function once, in depth-first discovery order.

Parameters
max_depthMaximum nesting depth to descend; 0 returns only the directly owned functions, a negative number means no limit
Returns
Unique list of embedded functions

>>> x = MX.sym("x")
>>> inner = Function("inner", [x], [sin(x)]).expand()
>>> middle = Function("middle", [x], [inner(x) + 1])
>>> outer = Function("outer", [x], [middle.call([x], False, True)[0]*2])
>>> [g.name() for g in outer.find_functions()]
['middle', 'inner']
>>> [g.name() for g in outer.find_functions(0)]
['middle']
>>> outer.find_function("inner")
Function(inner:(i0)->(o0) SXFunction)
>>> Function("o2", [x], [middle(x)*2]).get_function()
['inner']


>> x = MX.sym('x');
>> inner = Function('inner', {x}, {sin(x)}).expand();
>> middle = Function('middle', {x}, {inner(x) + 1});
>> res = middle.call({x}, false, true); outer = Function('outer', {x}, {res{1}*2});
>> fs = outer.find_functions(); cellfun(@(g) disp(g.name()), fs)
middle
inner
>> fs = outer.find_functions(0); disp(fs{1}.name())
middle
>> outer.find_function('inner')
inner:(i0)->(o0) SXFunction
>> Function('o2', {x}, {middle(x)*2}).get_function()
inner


julia> x = ca.MX.sym("x");
julia> inner = ca.expand(ca.Function("inner", [x], [sin(x)]));
julia> middle = ca.Function("middle", [x], [inner(x) + 1]);
julia> outer = ca.Function("outer", [x], [ca.call(middle, [x], false, true)[1] * 2]);
julia> [ca.name(g) for g in ca.find_functions(outer)]
2-element Vector{String}:
 "middle"
 "inner"
julia> [ca.name(g) for g in ca.find_functions(outer, 0)]
1-element Vector{String}:
 "middle"
julia> ca.find_function(outer, "inner")
Function(inner:(i0)->(o0) SXFunction)
julia> ca.get_function(ca.Function("o2", [x], [middle(x) * 2]))
1-element Vector{String}:

◆ fix_name()

static std::string casadi::Function::fix_name ( const std::string &  name)
static

Runs of non-alphanumeric characters become a single underscore, a leading non-letter gets an "a" prepended, the empty string becomes "unnamed" and a keyword gets "1" appended. A valid name is returned unchanged.

>>> Function.fix_name("my func!"), Function.fix_name("1f"), Function.fix_name("")
('my_func_', 'a1f', 'unnamed')
>>> Function.fix_name("jac"), Function.fix_name("ok")
('jac1', 'ok')


>> Function.fix_name('my func!'), Function.fix_name('1f')
my_func_
a1f
>> Function.fix_name(''), Function.fix_name('jac')
unnamed
jac1
>> Function.fix_name('ok')
ok


julia> F = ca.Function;
julia> F.fix_name("my func!"), F.fix_name("1f"), F.fix_name("")
("my_func_", "a1f", "unnamed")
julia> F.fix_name("jac"), F.fix_name("ok")
("jac1", "ok")

◆ fold()

Function casadi::Function::fold ( casadi_int  N,
const Dict &  opts = Dict() 
) const

as inputs

The first n_accum inputs are accumulators: in every step they take the value of the first n_accum outputs of the previous step. For a step f:(x, u)->(x_next, y) the result is F:(x0, U)->(X, Y) with U = horzcat(u0, ..., u_(N-1)), X = horzcat(x1, ..., x_N), Y = horzcat(y0, ..., y_(N-1)) and x_(k+1), y_k = f(x_k, u_k). Non-accumulated inputs and all outputs are stacked horizontally over the N steps; the accumulator inputs keep their original shape. An accumulator output may be wider than its input (e.g. 1-by-2 feeding a 1-by-1 input): only the last columns are fed back.

Compared with writing the loop in the host language the construction is much faster and the graph is a tower of nested unrolled loops of at most base iterations (option "base", default 10; -1 unrolls completely). This keeps memory use proportional to base rather than N at a small evaluation cost.

The overloads differ in how accumulators are selected: a count n_accum (default 1), or explicit index/name lists accum_in and accum_out of equal length that may pick arbitrary inputs and outputs. The unnamed overload names the result "mapaccum_" + name(). fold is mapaccum returning only the final accumulator state.

Parameters
nameName of the result
NNumber of steps, must be positive
n_accumNumber of leading inputs/outputs that are accumulated; at most min(n_in, n_out)
opts"base" plus options for the resulting MXFunction

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); u = SX.sym("u")
>>> step = Function("step", [x, u], [x + u, x*u], ["x", "u"], ["x_next", "y"])
>>> F = step.mapaccum(3)
>>> F
Function(mapaccum_step:(x,u[1x3])->(x_next[1x3],y[1x3]) MXFunction)
>>> F(1, DM([[1, 2, 3]]))
(DM([[2, 4, 7]]), DM([[1, 4, 12]]))
>>> step.mapaccum("F", 3, ["x"], ["x_next"])
Function(F:(x,u[1x3])->(x_next[1x3],y[1x3]) MXFunction)
>>> step.fold(3)
Function(fold_step:(x,u[1x3])->(x_next,y[1x3]) MXFunction)
>>> step.fold(3)(1, DM([[1, 2, 3]]))
(DM(7), DM([[1, 4, 12]]))
>>> try:
...   step.mapaccum(0)
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("mapaccum: N must be positive" in str(e))
True


>> x = SX.sym('x'); u = SX.sym('u');

◆ forward()

Function casadi::Function::forward ( casadi_int  nfwd) const

The result is named fwd<nfwd>_<name> and has n_in + n_out + n_in inputs: the original inputs, the original outputs (out_<name>, read only when uses_output() is true) and the forward seeds fwd_<input>, each with nfwd times the columns of the input. Its n_out outputs fwd_<output> are the forward sensitivities, nfwd stacked horizontally. Uses the class' own forward mode when available ("enable_forward"), finite differences when "enable_fd" is set, or otherwise the full Jacobian; raises "Derivatives cannot be calculated" when none applies. Results are cached while alive. nfwd must be positive.

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); y = SX.sym("y", 2)
>>> f = Function("f", [x, y], [x*y, sin(x)], ["x", "y"], ["r", "s"])
>>> F = f.forward(1)
>>> print(F)
fwd1_f:(x,y[2],out_r[2x1,0nz],out_s[1x1,0nz],fwd_x,fwd_y[2])->(...) SXFunction
>>> F(1, [2, 3], 0, 0, 1, [0, 0])
(DM([2, 3]), DM(0.540302))
>>> print(f.forward(2))
fwd2_f:(x,y[2],out_r[2x1,0nz],out_s[1x1,0nz],fwd_x[1x2],fwd_y[2x2])->(...) SXFunction


>> x = SX.sym('x'); y = SX.sym('y', 2);
>> f = Function('f', {x, y}, {x*y, sin(x)}, {'x', 'y'}, {'r', 's'});
>> F = f.forward(1); disp(F)
fwd1_f:(x,y[2],out_r[2x1,0nz],out_s[1x1,0nz],fwd_x,fwd_y[2])->(...) SXFunction
>> [fwd_r, fwd_s] = F(1, [2; 3], 0, 0, 1, [0; 0]); disp(fwd_r), disp(fwd_s)
[2, 3]
0.540302
>> f.forward(2)
fwd2_f:(x,y[2],out_r[2x1,0nz],out_s[1x1,0nz],fwd_x[1x2],fwd_y[2x2])->(...) SXFunction


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x"); y = ca.SX.sym("y", 2);
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x, y], [x*y, sin(x)], ["x", "y"], ["r", "s"]);
julia> F = ca.forward(f, 1);
julia> F
Function(fwd1_f:(x,y[2],out_r[2x1,0nz],out_s[1x1,0nz],fwd_x,fwd_y[2])->(fwd_r[2],fwd_s) SXFunction)
julia> F(1, [2, 3], 0, 0, 1, [0, 0])
([2, 3], 0.540302)
julia> F2 = ca.forward(f, 2);
julia> ca.name(F2), ca.size_in(F2, 4), ca.size_in(F2, 5)
("fwd2_f", (1, 2), (2, 2))

See also
reverse, jacobian, factory

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1wq

◆ generate() [1/2]

std::string casadi::Function::generate ( const Dict &  opts = Dict()) const

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1wv

◆ generate() [2/2]

std::string casadi::Function::generate ( const std::string &  fname,
const Dict &  opts = Dict() 
) const

Writes fname (a ".c" suffix is added when missing; the base name must satisfy check_name) in the current directory and returns the file name. The generated file exports, for a function named f, the evaluation routine int f(const casadi_real** arg, casadi_real** res, casadi_int* iw, casadi_real* w, int mem) plus f_n_in, f_n_out, f_name_in, f_name_out, f_sparsity_in, f_sparsity_out, f_work, f_checkout, f_release, f_incref, f_decref and f_default_in. The arrays hold the nonzeros of each input and output in column-major order; work vector sizes come from f_work. Functions embedded in the graph (solvers, maps, ...) are generated too when they support code generation; otherwise an error is raised. The same file can hold several functions via the CodeGenerator class.

Parameters
fnameFile name, e.g. "f.c" or "f"
optsCode generator options: "with_header" (also write a .h file), "main" (add a main() reading inputs from a file written by generate_in), "casadi_real" (scalar type, default "double"), "casadi_int" (integer type), "with_mem" (simplified memory API), "mex", "cpp", "indent", "prefix", "verbose", "with_export", "with_import", "include_math", "infinity", "nan", "real_min", "unroll_args", "codegen_scalars", "avoid_stack", "thread_safe", "force_canonical"

>>> import os
>>> x = SX.sym("x"); y = SX.sym("y", 2)
>>> f = Function("f", [x, y], [x*y, sin(x)], ["x", "y"], ["r", "s"])
>>> fname = f.generate("f.c")
>>> fname, os.path.isfile("f.c")
('f.c', True)
>>> src = open("f.c").read()
>>> print("int f(const casadi_real** arg, casadi_real** res" in src, "f_n_in" in src)
True True
>>> os.remove("f.c")
>>> f.generate("g", {"with_header": True}), sorted(os.listdir(".")).count("g.h")
('g.c', 1)
>>> os.remove("g.c"); os.remove("g.h")


>> x = SX.sym('x'); y = SX.sym('y', 2);
>> f = Function('f', {x, y}, {x*y, sin(x)}, {'x', 'y'}, {'r', 's'});
>> fname = f.generate('f.c'); disp(fname), disp(isfile('f.c'))
f.c
1
>> src = fileread('f.c');
>> disp(contains(src, 'int f(const casadi_real** arg, casadi_real** res'))
1
>> contains(src, 'f_n_in')
1
>> delete('f.c');
>> f.generate('g', struct('with_header', true)), isfile('g.h')
g.c
1
>> delete('g.c'); delete('g.h');


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x"); y = ca.SX.sym("y", 2);
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x, y], [x*y, sin(x)], ["x", "y"], ["r", "s"]);
julia> fname = ca.generate(f, "f.c")
"f.c"
julia> isfile("f.c")
true
julia> src = read("f.c", String);
julia> occursin("int f(const casadi_real** arg, casadi_real** res", src)
true
julia> occursin("f_n_in", src)
true
julia> rm("f.c")
julia> ca.generate(f, "g", Dict("with_header" => true)), isfile("g.h")
("g.c", true)
julia> rm("g.c"); rm("g.h")

See also
CodeGenerator, generate_in, generate_out, jit, external

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1wu

Examples
SX/generateCode.py.

◆ generate_dependencies()

std::string casadi::Function::generate_dependencies ( const std::string &  fname,
const Dict &  opts = Dict() 
) const

Only defined for solver classes built on an oracle (nlpsol, rootfinder, integrator, ...): writes code for the helper functions (e.g. nlp_f, nlp_jac_g) into fname and returns the file name. Other classes raise an error. The library compiled from this file can be loaded with external and passed back to the solver constructor in place of the symbolic problem.

Parameters
fnameFile name
optsCode generator options, as for generate
See also
generate, external

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1ww

◆ generate_in() [1/2]

std::vector<DM> casadi::Function::generate_in ( const std::string &  fname)

With arguments, writes the nonzeros of arg (one value per line, in input order) to fname; this is the input format read by code generated with option "main". Without arguments, reads such a file back into a list of DM with the input sparsities. The arguments follow the shape rules of call (scalars are broadcast); a file with the wrong number of values raises a dimension error.

>>> import os
>>> f = Function("f", [SX.sym("x"), SX.sym("y", 2)], [SX(0)])
>>> f.generate_in("f_in.txt", [1, [2, 3]])
>>> open("f_in.txt").read().split()
['1.0000000000000000e+00', '2.0000000000000000e+00', '3.0000000000000000e+00']
>>> f.generate_in("f_in.txt")
[DM(1), DM([2, 3])]
>>> os.remove("f_in.txt")


>> f = Function('f', {SX.sym('x'), SX.sym('y', 2)}, {SX(0)});
>> f.generate_in('f_in.txt', {1, [2; 3]});
>> fileread('f_in.txt')
1.0000000000000000e+00
2.0000000000000000e+00
3.0000000000000000e+00
>> arg = f.generate_in('f_in.txt'); disp(arg{1}), disp(arg{2})
1
[2, 3]
>> delete('f_in.txt');


julia> f = ca.Function("f", [ca.SX.sym("x"), ca.SX.sym("y", 2)], [ca.SX(0)]);
julia> ca.generate_in(f, "f_in.txt", [1, [2, 3]])
julia> split(read("f_in.txt", String))
3-element Vector{SubString{String}}:
 "1.0000000000000000e+00"
 "2.0000000000000000e+00"
 "3.0000000000000000e+00"
julia> ca.generate_in(f, "f_in.txt")
2-element Vector{DM}:
 DM(1)
 DM([2, 3])
julia> rm("f_in.txt")

◆ get_function() [1/2]

std::vector<std::string> casadi::Function::get_function ( ) const

For an SXFunction or MXFunction: the functions called in its graph (an MXFunction called with MX arguments is inlined and does not appear; SX functions, solvers and calls made with never_inline do); for a map: the mapped function; for a solver: its helper functions (e.g. nlp_f, nlp_jac_g for nlpsol, which can also be code-generated with generate_dependencies). Empty for leaf classes. Note that has_function only answers true for solver objects; for SX and MX functions it is false even for names in this list.

>>> x = MX.sym("x")
>>> f = Function("f", [x], [sin(x)]).expand()
>>> g = Function("g", [x], [f(x) + 1])
>>> g.get_function(), g.has_function("f")
(['f'], True)
>>> g.get_function("f")
Function(f:(i0)->(o0) SXFunction)
>>> try:
...   g.get_function("h")
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("No such function 'h'" in str(e))
True


>> x = MX.sym('x');
>> f = Function('f', {x}, {sin(x)}).expand();
>> g = Function('g', {x}, {f(x) + 1});
>> g.get_function(), g.has_function('f')
f
1
>> g.get_function('f')
f:(i0)->(o0) SXFunction
>> try, g.get_function('h'); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'No such function')); end
1


julia> x = ca.MX.sym("x");
julia> f = ca.expand(ca.Function("f", [x], [sin(x)]));
julia> g = ca.Function("g", [x], [f(x) + 1]);
julia> ca.get_function(g), ca.has_function(g, "f")
(["f"], true)
julia> ca.get_function(g, "f")
Function(f:(i0)->(o0) SXFunction)
julia> try
           ca.get_function(g, "h")
       catch e
           println(occursin("No such function 'h'", sprint(showerror, e)))
       end
true

See also
has_function, find_functions, oracle

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1y3

◆ has_free()

bool casadi::Function::has_free ( ) const

Only possible for SXFunction and MXFunction constructed with "allow_free". Such a function can be called symbolically but not numerically, expanded, code-generated or differentiated into a numerically usable form.

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); p = SX.sym("p")
>>> f = Function("f", [x], [x*p], {"allow_free": True})
>>> f.has_free(), f.get_free(), f.free_sx()
(True, ['p'], [SX(p)])
>>> try:
...   f(1)
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("since variables [p] are free" in str(e))
True
>>> f(SX.sym("q"))
SX((q*p))
>>> Function("g", [x], [x]).has_free()
False


>> x = SX.sym('x'); p = SX.sym('p');
>> f = Function('f', {x}, {x*p}, struct('allow_free', true));
>> disp(f.has_free()), disp(f.get_free()), fr = f.free_sx(); disp(fr{1})
1
p
p
>> try, f(1); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'variables [p] are free')); end
1
>> f(SX.sym('q'))
(q*p)
>> Function('g', {x}, {x}).has_free()
0


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x"); p = ca.SX.sym("p");
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x], [x*p], Dict("allow_free" => true));
julia> ca.has_free(f), ca.get_free(f), ca.free_sx(f)
(true, ["p"], SX[p])
julia> try
           f(1)
       catch e
           println(occursin("since variables [p] are free", sprint(showerror, e)))
       end
true
julia> f(ca.SX.sym("q"))
SX((q*p))
julia> ca.has_free(ca.Function("g", [x], [x]))
false

See also
get_free, free_sx, free_mx

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1x8

◆ has_spfwd()

bool casadi::Function::has_spfwd ( ) const

sparsity propagation

Both are true for SXFunction and MXFunction; classes without it fall back to dense Jacobian sparsity patterns.

>>> f = Function("f", [SX.sym("x")], [SX(0)])
>>> f.has_spfwd(), f.has_sprev()
(True, True)


>> f = Function('f', {SX.sym('x')}, {SX(0)});
>> fprintf('%d %d\n', f.has_spfwd(), f.has_sprev())
1 1


julia> f = ca.Function("f", [ca.SX.sym("x")], [ca.SX(0)]);
julia> ca.has_spfwd(f), ca.has_sprev(f)
(true, true)

See also
jac_sparsity

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1xl

◆ if_else()

static Function casadi::Function::if_else ( const std::string &  name,
const Function &  f_true,
const Function &  f_false,
const Dict &  opts = Dict() 
)
static

a condition input

The result takes the condition as first input followed by the inputs of the branches; a nonzero condition (truncated to integer) selects f_true, zero selects f_false. Only the selected branch is evaluated numerically. The signature is taken from f_false (see conditional).

>>> x = SX.sym("x")
>>> f = Function.if_else("f", Function("t", [x], [x**2]), Function("e", [x], [x]))
>>> f
Function(f:(i0,i1)->(o0) Switch)
>>> f(1, 3), f(0, 3), f(5, 3)
(DM(9), DM(3), DM(9))


>> x = SX.sym('x');
>> f_true = Function('t', {x}, {x^2}); f_false = Function('e', {x}, {x});
>> f = Function.if_else('f', f_true, f_false); disp(f)
f:(i0,i1)->(o0) Switch
>> [f(1, 3), f(0, 3), f(5, 3)]
[[9, 3, 9]]

◆ is_a()

bool casadi::Function::is_a ( const std::string &  type,
bool  recursive = true 
) const

type is a class name as printed after the signature: "SXFunction", "MXFunction", "Map", "Switch", "External", "Nlpsol", "Conic", "Integrator", "Rootfinder" and the plugin names (e.g. "Ipopt"). With recursive true (default) base classes match as well ("Nlpsol" for an Ipopt solver, "Map" for a ThreadMap, "FunctionInternal" for everything).

>>> f = Function("f", [SX.sym("x")], [SX(0)])
>>> f.is_a("SXFunction"), f.is_a("MXFunction"), f.class_name()
(True, False, 'SXFunction')
>>> f.map(2, "thread").is_a("Map"), f.map(2, "thread").is_a("Map", False)
(True, False)


>> f = Function('f', {SX.sym('x')}, {SX(0)});
>> fprintf('%d %d %s\n', f.is_a('SXFunction'), f.is_a('MXFunction'), f.class_name())
1 0 SXFunction
>> F = f.map(2, 'thread'); fprintf('%d %d\n', F.is_a('Map'), F.is_a('Map', false))
1 0

◆ is_diff_in() [1/2]

std::vector<bool> casadi::Function::is_diff_in ( ) const

All true by default; set with options "is_diff_in" and "is_diff_out". A non-differentiable input or output is skipped by jacobian, forward, reverse and factory: the corresponding derivative blocks are all-zero sparse. The signature printout marks them with a '#'. Without argument, the list for all inputs or outputs is returned.

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); n = SX.sym("n")
>>> f = Function("f", [x, n], [x*n], ["x", "n"], ["r"], {"is_diff_in": [True, False]})
>>> f
Function(f:(x,#n)->(r) SXFunction)
>>> f.is_diff_in(), f.is_diff_in(1), f.is_diff_out()
([True, False], False, [True])
>>> f.jacobian()
Function(jac_f:(x,#n,out_r[1x1,0nz])->(jac_r_x,#jac_r_n[1x1,0nz]) SXFunction)


>> x = SX.sym('x'); n = SX.sym('n');
>> opts = struct('is_diff_in', [true, false]);
>> f = Function('f', {x, n}, {x*n}, {'x', 'n'}, {'r'}, opts); disp(f)
f:(x,#n)->(r) SXFunction
>> f.is_diff_in(), f.is_diff_in(1), f.is_diff_out()
1 0
0
1
>> f.jacobian()
jac_f:(x,#n,out_r[1x1,0nz])->(jac_r_x,#jac_r_n[1x1,0nz]) SXFunction


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x"); n = ca.SX.sym("n");
julia> opts = Dict("is_diff_in" => [true, false]);
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x, n], [x*n], ["x", "n"], ["r"], opts);
julia> f
Function(f:(x,#n)->(r) SXFunction)
julia> ca.is_diff_in(f), ca.is_diff_in(f, 1), ca.is_diff_out(f)
(Bool[1, 0], false, Bool[1])
julia> ca.jacobian(f)
Function(jac_f:(x,#n,out_r[1x1,0nz])->(jac_r_x,#jac_r_n[1x1,0nz]) SXFunction)

◆ jac_sparsity() [1/2]

const std::vector<Sparsity>& casadi::Function::jac_sparsity ( bool  compact = false) const

One pattern per (output, input) pair, ordered by output first, as in jacobian. Patterns are computed by sparsity propagation the first time and cached.

Parameters
compactWhen true, the pattern is over the nonzeros of output and input (nnz_out-by-nnz_in) rather than over all elements (numel_out-by-numel_in)

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); D = SX.sym("D", Sparsity.diag(2))
>>> f = Function("f", [x, D], [x*D, sin(x)])
>>> f.jac_sparsity()
[Sparsity(4x1,2nz), Sparsity(4x4,2nz), Sparsity(1x1), Sparsity(1x4,0nz)]
>>> f.jac_sparsity(True)
[Sparsity(2x1), Sparsity(2x2,2nz), Sparsity(1x1), Sparsity(1x2,0nz)]


>> x = SX.sym('x'); D = SX.sym('D', Sparsity.diag(2));
>> f = Function('f', {x, D}, {x*D, sin(x)});
>> sp = f.jac_sparsity(); disp(numel(sp)), cellfun(@disp, sp)
4
4x1,2nz
4x4,2nz
1x1
1x4,0nz
>> cellfun(@disp, f.jac_sparsity(true))
2x1
2x2,2nz
1x1
1x2,0nz


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x"); D = ca.SX.sym("D", ca.Sparsity.diag(2));
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x, D], [x*D, sin(x)]);
julia> ca.jac_sparsity(f)
4-element Vector{Sparsity}:
 Sparsity(4x1,2nz)
 Sparsity(4x4,2nz)
 Sparsity(1x1)
 Sparsity(1x4,0nz)
julia> ca.jac_sparsity(f, true)
4-element Vector{Sparsity}:
 Sparsity(2x1)
 Sparsity(2x2,2nz)
 Sparsity(1x1)
 Sparsity(1x2,0nz)

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1ws

◆ jacobian()

Function casadi::Function::jacobian ( ) const

The result is named jac_<name> and takes all inputs followed by all outputs (named out_<output>); the output values are only read when uses_output() is true and may be passed as 0 otherwise. It returns one block per (output, input) pair, ordered by output first: for f:(x,y)->(r,s) the outputs are jac_r_x, jac_r_y, jac_s_x, jac_s_y. Blocks involving a non-differentiable input or output are all-zero sparse. Each block is a size_out(o)-times-size_in(i) matrix where the output and input are vectorized column-major; blocks that are structurally zero have an all-zero sparsity pattern.

The result is cached: calling jacobian again returns the same object as long as it is alive. The derivative itself is built by symbolic AD for SX and MX functions, by the solver's own sensitivity method for solver objects, or raises "Derivatives cannot be calculated" for classes without any derivative support. For a single block, factory("...", ..., ["jac:r:x"]) avoids computing the others.

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); y = SX.sym("y", 2)
>>> f = Function("f", [x, y], [x*y, sin(x)], ["x", "y"], ["r", "s"])
>>> J = f.jacobian()
>>> print(J)
jac_f:(x,y[2],out_r[2x1,0nz],out_s[1x1,0nz])->(jac_r_x[2],jac_r_y[2x2,2nz],...) SXFunction
>>> J(1, [2, 3], 0, 0)
(DM([2, 3]), DM(
[[1, 00],

◆ jit() [1/2]

static Function casadi::Function::jit ( const std::string &  name,
const std::string &  body,
const std::vector< std::string > &  name_in,
const std::vector< std::string > &  name_out,
const Dict &  opts = Dict() 
)
static

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1v2

Create a just-in-time compiled function from a C language string

body is the body of a C function that is compiled with the "compiler" plugin (option "compiler", default "shell" i.e. the system C compiler on most builds, or "clang") and loaded as an external function. This needs a working C compiler at run time; the source and library files are removed afterwards unless "jit_cleanup" is false.

Inside the body, the nonzeros of input k are available through a pointer named name_in[k] (type const casadi_real*) and the nonzeros of output k must be written through a pointer named name_out[k] (type casadi_real*), in column-major order of the sparsity pattern. casadi_real is double unless the code generator is told otherwise. By default (option "buffered": true) inputs and outputs are copied through a work vector, so the pointers are never null; with "buffered": false a null input means all-zero and a null output may be skipped. Write "return 1;" to signal an evaluation failure.

The result has no derivatives unless option "enable_fd": true (finite differences) is passed or a C body for the Jacobian is supplied with option "jac" (and "hess" for its Jacobian).

Parameters
nameFunction name
bodyC code of the function body, without the enclosing braces
name_inInput names, also the C variable names
name_outOutput names, also the C variable names
sparsity_inSparsity of each input; all scalar when omitted
sparsity_outSparsity of each output; all scalar when omitted
optsOptions; "compiler", "jit_options", "buffered", "jac", "hess" and the common Function options

Example (not run here because it invokes a compiler): f = Function.jit("f", "r[0] = x[0]*x[0] + y[0];", ["x", "y"], ["r"]) gives a function f:(x,y)->(r) that evaluates like any other Function.

See also
external

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1v3

const std::vector< Sparsity > &  sparsity_in,

◆ map() [1/5]

Function casadi::Function::map ( casadi_int  n,
const std::string &  parallelization,
casadi_int  max_num_threads 
) const

◆ map() [2/5]

Function casadi::Function::map ( casadi_int  n,
const std::string &  parallelization = "serial" 
) const

For f:(a, p)->(s) the result is F:(A, P)->(S) with A = horzcat(a0, ..., a_(n-1)), the same for P, and S = horzcat(f(a0, p0), ..., f(a_(n-1), p_(n-1))). Every input and output is widened to n times its number of columns; when calling, a non-widened argument is repeated for all evaluations. The graph size and construction time are independent of n. For n == 1 the function itself is returned; n < 1 raises "Degenerate map operation".

Parameters
nNumber of evaluations
parallelizationHow the evaluations are run: "serial" (a Map node evaluated in a loop; cached, so repeated calls return the same object), "openmp" (OpenMP parallel loop, requires an OpenMP build), "thread" (std::thread per evaluation), "unroll" (n explicit calls in an MXFunction), "inline" (like unroll with the calls inlined). Other values raise an error.
max_num_threadsWith "thread" or "openmp": split the n evaluations into at most this many parallel chunks, each a serial map

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); p = SX.sym("p")
>>> f = Function("f", [x, p], [p*x**2], ["x", "p"], ["y"])
>>> F = f.map(3)
>>> F
Function(map3_f:(x[1x3],p[1x3])->(y[1x3]) Map)
>>> F(DM([[1, 2, 3]]), 2)
DM([[2, 8, 18]])
>>> f.map(4, "thread", 2)
Function(threadmap2_map2_f:(x[1x4],p[1x4])->(y[1x4]) ThreadMap)
>>> f.map(3, "unroll")
Function(f_3:(x[1x3],p[1x3])->(y[1x3]) MXFunction)
>>> f.map(1) is f, f.map(1).name()
(False, 'f')


>> x = SX.sym('x'); p = SX.sym('p');
>> f = Function('f', {x, p}, {p*x^2}, {'x', 'p'}, {'y'});
>> F = f.map(3); disp(F)
map3_f:(x[1x3],p[1x3])->(y[1x3]) Map
>> F(DM([1, 2, 3]), 2)
[[2, 8, 18]]
>> f.map(4, 'thread', 2)
threadmap2_map2_f:(x[1x4],p[1x4])->(y[1x4]) ThreadMap
>> f.map(3, 'unroll')
f_3:(x[1x3],p[1x3])->(y[1x3]) MXFunction
>> f.map(1).name()
f


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x"); p = ca.SX.sym("p");
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x, p], [p*x^2], ["x", "p"], ["y"]);
julia> F = f.map(3);
julia> F
Function(map3_f:(x[1x3],p[1x3])->(y[1x3]) Map)
julia> F([1 2 3], 2)
DM([[2, 8, 18]])
julia> f.map(4, "thread", 2)
Function(threadmap2_map2_f:(x[1x4],p[1x4])->(y[1x4]) ThreadMap)
julia> f.map(3, "unroll")
Function(f_3:(x[1x3],p[1x3])->(y[1x3]) MXFunction)
julia> f.map(1) === f, ca.name(f.map(1))
(false, "f")

See also
mapaccum, mapsum, call

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1wj

◆ map() [3/5]

Function casadi::Function::map ( casadi_int  n,
const std::vector< bool > &  reduce_in,
const std::vector< bool > &  reduce_out = std::vector< bool >(),
const Dict &  opts = Dict() 
) const

Inputs listed in reduce_in are not widened: the same value is used in all n evaluations. Outputs listed in reduce_out are summed over the evaluations instead of stacked. The index and name overloads build an MXFunction around a plain map (parallelization as in map; opts is currently ignored). The boolean-mask overload creates a dedicated serial MapSum node wrapped in an MXFunction built with opts; masks must have length n_in and n_out (an empty reduce_out means no reduction).

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); p = SX.sym("p")
>>> f = Function("f", [x, p], [p*x, x], ["x", "p"], ["y", "z"])
>>> F = f.map("F", "serial", 3, ["p"], ["y"])
>>> F
Function(F:(x[1x3],p)->(y,z[1x3]) MXFunction)
>>> F(DM([[1, 2, 3]]), 2)
(DM(12), DM([[1, 2, 3]]))
>>> f.map(3, [False, True], [True, False])(DM([[1, 2, 3]]), 2)
(DM(12), DM([[1, 2, 3]]))


>> x = SX.sym('x'); p = SX.sym('p');
>> f = Function('f', {x, p}, {p*x, x}, {'x', 'p'}, {'y', 'z'});
>> F = f.map('F', 'serial', 3, {'p'}, {'y'}); disp(F)
F:(x[1x3],p)->(y,z[1x3]) MXFunction
>> [y, z] = F(DM([1, 2, 3]), 2); disp(y), disp(z)
12
[[1, 2, 3]]
>> G = f.map(3, [false, true], [true, false]);
>> [y, z] = G(DM([1, 2, 3]), 2); disp(y), disp(z)
12
[[1, 2, 3]]


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x"); p = ca.SX.sym("p");
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x, p], [p*x, x], ["x", "p"], ["y", "z"]);
julia> F = f.map("F", "serial", 3, ["p"], ["y"]);
julia> F
Function(F:(x[1x3],p)->(y,z[1x3]) MXFunction)
julia> F([1 2 3], 2)
(12, [[1, 2, 3]])
julia> f.map(3, [false, true], [true, false])([1 2 3], 2)
(12, [[1, 2, 3]])
