◆ mapaccum() [1/5]

Function casadi::Function::mapaccum ( casadi_int  N,
const Dict &  opts = Dict() 
) const

as inputs

The first n_accum inputs are accumulators: in every step they take the value of the first n_accum outputs of the previous step. For a step f:(x, u)->(x_next, y) the result is F:(x0, U)->(X, Y) with U = horzcat(u0, ..., u_(N-1)), X = horzcat(x1, ..., x_N), Y = horzcat(y0, ..., y_(N-1)) and x_(k+1), y_k = f(x_k, u_k). Non-accumulated inputs and all outputs are stacked horizontally over the N steps; the accumulator inputs keep their original shape. An accumulator output may be wider than its input (e.g. 1-by-2 feeding a 1-by-1 input): only the last columns are fed back.

Compared with writing the loop in the host language the construction is much faster and the graph is a tower of nested unrolled loops of at most base iterations (option "base", default 10; -1 unrolls completely). This keeps memory use proportional to base rather than N at a small evaluation cost.

The overloads differ in how accumulators are selected: a count n_accum (default 1), or explicit index/name lists accum_in and accum_out of equal length that may pick arbitrary inputs and outputs. The unnamed overload names the result "mapaccum_" + name(). fold is mapaccum returning only the final accumulator state.

Parameters
nameName of the result
NNumber of steps, must be positive
n_accumNumber of leading inputs/outputs that are accumulated; at most min(n_in, n_out)
opts"base" plus options for the resulting MXFunction

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); u = SX.sym("u")
>>> step = Function("step", [x, u], [x + u, x*u], ["x", "u"], ["x_next", "y"])
>>> F = step.mapaccum(3)
>>> F
Function(mapaccum_step:(x,u[1x3])->(x_next[1x3],y[1x3]) MXFunction)
>>> F(1, DM([[1, 2, 3]]))
(DM([[2, 4, 7]]), DM([[1, 4, 12]]))
>>> step.mapaccum("F", 3, ["x"], ["x_next"])
Function(F:(x,u[1x3])->(x_next[1x3],y[1x3]) MXFunction)
>>> step.fold(3)
Function(fold_step:(x,u[1x3])->(x_next,y[1x3]) MXFunction)
>>> step.fold(3)(1, DM([[1, 2, 3]]))
(DM(7), DM([[1, 4, 12]]))
>>> try:
...   step.mapaccum(0)
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("mapaccum: N must be positive" in str(e))
True


>> x = SX.sym('x'); u = SX.sym('u');
>> step = Function('step', {x, u}, {x + u, x*u}, {'x', 'u'}, {'x_next', 'y'});
>> F = step.mapaccum(3); disp(F)
mapaccum_step:(x,u[1x3])->(x_next[1x3],y[1x3]) MXFunction
>> [X, Y] = F(1, DM([1, 2, 3])); disp(X), disp(Y)
[[2, 4, 7]]
[[1, 4, 12]]
>> step.mapaccum('F', 3, {'x'}, {'x_next'})
F:(x,u[1x3])->(x_next[1x3],y[1x3]) MXFunction
>> G = step.fold(3); disp(G)
fold_step:(x,u[1x3])->(x_next,y[1x3]) MXFunction
>> [X, Y] = G(1, DM([1, 2, 3])); disp(X), disp(Y)
7
[[1, 4, 12]]
>> try, step.mapaccum(0); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'N must be positive')); end
1


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x"); u = ca.SX.sym("u");
julia> step = ca.Function("step", [x, u], [x + u, x*u], ["x", "u"], ["x_next", "y"]);
julia> F = ca.mapaccum(step, 3);
julia> F
Function(mapaccum_step:(x,u[1x3])->(x_next[1x3],y[1x3]) MXFunction)
julia> F(1, [1 2 3])
([[2, 4, 7]], [[1, 4, 12]])
julia> ca.mapaccum(step, "F", 3, ["x"], ["x_next"])
Function(F:(x,u[1x3])->(x_next[1x3],y[1x3]) MXFunction)
julia> ca.fold(step, 3)
Function(fold_step:(x,u[1x3])->(x_next,y[1x3]) MXFunction)
julia> ca.fold(step, 3)(1, [1 2 3])
(7, [[1, 4, 12]])
julia> try
           ca.mapaccum(step, 0)
       catch e
           println(occursin("mapaccum: N must be positive", sprint(showerror, e)))
       end
true

See also
map, fold

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1wi

◆ mapaccum() [2/5]

>>> try:
...   step.mapaccum(0)
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("mapaccum: N must be positive" in str(e))

◆ mapaccum() [3/5]

Function casadi::Function::mapaccum ( const std::string &  name,
casadi_int  N,
const Dict &  opts = Dict() 
) const

as inputs

The first n_accum inputs are accumulators: in every step they take the value of the first n_accum outputs of the previous step. For a step f:(x, u)->(x_next, y) the result is F:(x0, U)->(X, Y) with U = horzcat(u0, ..., u_(N-1)), X = horzcat(x1, ..., x_N), Y = horzcat(y0, ..., y_(N-1)) and x_(k+1), y_k = f(x_k, u_k). Non-accumulated inputs and all outputs are stacked horizontally over the N steps; the accumulator inputs keep their original shape. An accumulator output may be wider than its input (e.g. 1-by-2 feeding a 1-by-1 input): only the last columns are fed back.

Compared with writing the loop in the host language the construction is much faster and the graph is a tower of nested unrolled loops of at most base iterations (option "base", default 10; -1 unrolls completely). This keeps memory use proportional to base rather than N at a small evaluation cost.

The overloads differ in how accumulators are selected: a count n_accum (default 1), or explicit index/name lists accum_in and accum_out of equal length that may pick arbitrary inputs and outputs. The unnamed overload names the result "mapaccum_" + name(). fold is mapaccum returning only the final accumulator state.

Parameters
nameName of the result
NNumber of steps, must be positive
n_accumNumber of leading inputs/outputs that are accumulated; at most min(n_in, n_out)
opts"base" plus options for the resulting MXFunction

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); u = SX.sym("u")
>>> step = Function("step", [x, u], [x + u, x*u], ["x", "u"], ["x_next", "y"])
>>> F = step.mapaccum(3)
>>> F
Function(mapaccum_step:(x,u[1x3])->(x_next[1x3],y[1x3]) MXFunction)
>>> F(1, DM([[1, 2, 3]]))
(DM([[2, 4, 7]]), DM([[1, 4, 12]]))
>>> step.mapaccum("F", 3, ["x"], ["x_next"])
Function(F:(x,u[1x3])->(x_next[1x3],y[1x3]) MXFunction)
>>> step.fold(3)
Function(fold_step:(x,u[1x3])->(x_next,y[1x3]) MXFunction)
>>> step.fold(3)(1, DM([[1, 2, 3]]))
(DM(7), DM([[1, 4, 12]]))
>>> try:
...   step.mapaccum(0)
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("mapaccum: N must be positive" in str(e))
True


>> x = SX.sym('x'); u = SX.sym('u');
>> step = Function('step', {x, u}, {x + u, x*u}, {'x', 'u'}, {'x_next', 'y'});
>> F = step.mapaccum(3); disp(F)
mapaccum_step:(x,u[1x3])->(x_next[1x3],y[1x3]) MXFunction
>> [X, Y] = F(1, DM([1, 2, 3])); disp(X), disp(Y)
[[2, 4, 7]]
[[1, 4, 12]]
>> step.mapaccum('F', 3, {'x'}, {'x_next'})

>> G = step.fold(3); disp(G)
fold_step:(x,u[1x3])->(x_next,y[1x3]) MXFunction
>> [X, Y] = G(1, DM([1, 2, 3])); disp(X), disp(Y)
7
[[1, 4, 12]]
>> try, step.mapaccum(0); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'N must be positive')); end
1

◆ mapsum()

std::vector<MX> casadi::Function::mapsum ( const std::vector< MX > &  x,
const std::string &  parallelization = "serial" 
) const

Each argument is either the input itself or n copies stacked horizontally (N-by-(n*M) for an N-by-M input); the largest such n sets the number of evaluations and non-stacked arguments are shared across evaluations. Returns the sum over the n evaluations of each output, as MX expressions calling an internal map-and-reduce function.

Parameters
xList of n_in() MX arguments
parallelization"serial" only; other values raise an error

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); y = SX.sym("y")
>>> f = Function("f", [x, y], [x*y, x - y], ["x", "y"], ["p", "d"])
>>> p, d = f.mapsum([MX.sym("a"), MX.sym("b", 1, 3)])
>>> p
MX(mapsum(a, b){0})
>>> f.mapsum([2, DM([[1, 2, 3]])])
[MX(mapsum(2, [[1, 2, 3]]){0}), MX(mapsum(2, [[1, 2, 3]]){1})]
>>> evalf(f.mapsum([2, DM([[1, 2, 3]])])[0])
DM(12)


>> x = SX.sym('x'); y = SX.sym('y');
>> f = Function('f', {x, y}, {x*y, x - y}, {'x', 'y'}, {'p', 'd'});
>> res = f.mapsum({MX.sym('a'), MX.sym('b', 1, 3)}); disp(res{1})
mapsum(a, b){0}
>> res = f.mapsum({2, DM([1, 2, 3])}); disp(res{1}), disp(res{2})
mapsum(2, [[1, 2, 3]]){0}
mapsum(2, [[1, 2, 3]]){1}
>> evalf(res{1})
12


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x"); y = ca.SX.sym("y");
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x, y], [x*y, x - y], ["x", "y"], ["p", "d"]);
julia> p, d = ca.mapsum(f, [ca.MX.sym("a"), ca.MX.sym("b", 1, 3)]);
julia> p
MX(mapsum(a, b){0})
julia> ca.mapsum(f, [2, ca.DM([1 2 3])])
2-element Vector{MX}:
 MX(mapsum(2, [[1, 2, 3]]){0})
 MX(mapsum(2, [[1, 2, 3]]){1})
julia> ca.evalf(ca.mapsum(f, [2, ca.DM([1 2 3])])[1])
DM(12)

◆ mx_in() [1/3]

const std::vector<MX> casadi::Function::mx_in ( ) const

Convenient for calling the function symbolically or for building a wrapper: f(f.mx_in()) is an MX expression of the outputs. Each call creates new symbols; they are not the symbols the function was constructed from. All inputs (no argument), one by index, or one by name.

>>> f = Function("f", [SX.sym("a"), SX.sym("B", Sparsity.diag(2))], [SX(0)],
...              ["a", "B"], ["r"])
>>> f.sx_in()
[SX(a), SX(
[[B_0, 00],
 [00, B_1]])]
>>> f.mx_in(1), f.mx_in("a").shape
(MX(B), (1, 1))
>>> f.sx_out(), f.mx_out(0)
([SX(r)], MX(r))


>> a = SX.sym('a'); B = SX.sym('B', Sparsity.diag(2));
>> f = Function('f', {a, B}, {SX(0)}, {'a', 'B'}, {'r'});
>> in = f.sx_in(); disp(in{1}), disp(in{2})
a
[[B_0, 00],
 [00, B_1]]
>> f.mx_in(1), size(f.mx_in('a'))
B
1 1
>> out = f.sx_out(); disp(out{1}), disp(f.mx_out(0))
r
r


julia> a = ca.SX.sym("a"); B = ca.SX.sym("B", ca.Sparsity.diag(2));
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [a, B], [ca.SX(0)], ["a", "B"], ["r"]);
julia> a_in, B_in = ca.sx_in(f);
julia> a_in
SX(a)
julia> B_in
SX(
[[B_0, 00],
 [00, B_1]])
julia> ca.mx_in(f, 1), size(ca.mx_in(f, "a"))
(B, (1, 1))
julia> ca.sx_out(f), ca.mx_out(f, 0)
(SX[r], r)

See also
sx_out, mx_out, sparsity_in

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1x4

julia> a = ca.SX.sym("a"); B = ca.SX.sym("B", ca.Sparsity.diag(2));
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [a, B], [ca.SX(0)], ["a", "B"], ["r"]);
julia> a_in, B_in = ca.sx_in(f);
julia> a_in
SX(a)
julia> B_in
SX(
[[B_0, 00],
 [00, B_1]])
julia> ca.mx_in(f, 1), size(ca.mx_in(f, "a"))
(B, (1, 1))
julia> ca.sx_out(f), ca.mx_out(f, 0)
(SX[r], r)

See also

◆ n_instructions()

casadi_int casadi::Function::n_instructions ( ) const

The algorithm is the sequence of operations evaluated for a call, including the input and output instructions; instruction_id, instruction_input, instruction_output, instruction_constant and instruction_MX inspect them by index. Other classes raise.

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); y = SX.sym("y")
>>> f = Function("f", [x, y], [x*y + 1])
>>> f.n_instructions()
6
>>> for k in range(f.n_instructions()):
...   print(f.instruction_id(k), f.instruction_input(k), f.instruction_output(k))
45 [0, 0] [0]
45 [1, 0] [1]
3 [0, 1] [0]
44 [] [1]
1 [0, 1] [0]
46 [0] [0, 0]
>>> f.instruction_id(3) == OP_CONST, f.instruction_constant(3)
(True, 1.0)


>> x = SX.sym('x'); y = SX.sym('y');
>> f = Function('f', {x, y}, {x*y + 1});
>> f.n_instructions()
6
>> arrayfun(@(k) f.instruction_id(k), 0:5)
45 45 3 44 1 46
>> f.instruction_input(2), f.instruction_output(2)
0 1
0
>> f.instruction_id(3) == OP_CONST, f.instruction_constant(3)
1
1


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x"); y = ca.SX.sym("y");
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x, y], [x*y + 1]);
julia> ca.n_instructions(f)
6
julia> for k in 0:ca.n_instructions(f)-1
           println(ca.instruction_id(f, k), " ", ca.instruction_input(f, k), " ",
                   ca.instruction_output(f, k))
       end
45 [0, 0] [0]
45 [1, 0] [1]
3 [0, 1] [0]
44 Int64[] [1]
1 [0, 1] [0]
46 [0] [0, 0]
julia> ca.instruction_id(f, 3) == ca.OP_CONST, ca.instruction_constant(f, 3)
(true, 1.0)

See also
instruction_id, instructions_sx, n_nodes

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1xe

◆ n_nodes()

casadi_int casadi::Function::n_nodes ( ) const

Counts the unique nodes of the output expressions, inputs included; a cheap measure of graph size for comparing transformations. Other classes raise.

>>> x = MX.sym("x")
>>> Function("f", [x], [sin(x) + sin(x)]).n_nodes()
4


>> x = MX.sym('x');
>> Function('f', {x}, {sin(x) + sin(x)}).n_nodes()
4


julia> x = ca.MX.sym("x");
julia> ca.n_nodes(ca.Function("f", [x], [sin(x) + sin(x)]))
4

See also
n_instructions

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1xd

◆ name_in() [1/2]

const std::vector<std::string>& casadi::Function::name_in ( ) const

Names come from the constructor; when none were given they are i0, i1, ... Solver objects have fixed schemes, e.g. x0, p, lbx, ubx, lbg, ubg, lam_x0, lam_g0 for nlpsol.

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); y = SX.sym("y")
>>> Function("f", [x, y], [x+y]).name_in()
['i0', 'i1']
>>> f = Function("f", [x, y], [x+y], ["x", "y"], ["r"])
>>> f.name_in(), f.name_in(1), f.name_out()
(['x', 'y'], 'y', ['r'])


>> x = SX.sym('x'); y = SX.sym('y');
>> Function('f', {x, y}, {x+y}).name_in()
i0
i1
>> f = Function('f', {x, y}, {x+y}, {'x', 'y'}, {'r'});
>> f.name_in(), f.name_in(1), f.name_out()
x
y
y
r


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x"); y = ca.SX.sym("y");
julia> ca.name_in(ca.Function("f", [x, y], [x + y]))
2-element Vector{String}:
 "i0"
 "i1"
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x, y], [x + y], ["x", "y"], ["r"]);
julia> ca.name_in(f), ca.name_in(f, 1), ca.name_out(f)
(["x", "y"], "y", ["r"])

See also
name_out, index_in, has_in

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1vg

◆ nnz_in() [1/3]

casadi_int casadi::Function::nnz_in ( ) const

Without argument, the sum over all inputs. The nonzero count is what numerical evaluation reads for the input; it is smaller than numel_in for sparse inputs.

>>> f = Function("f", [SX.sym("x", 2), SX.sym("D", Sparsity.diag(3))], [SX(1)])
>>> f.nnz_in(0), f.nnz_in(1), f.nnz_in()
(2, 3, 5)
>>> f.numel_in(0), f.numel_in(1), f.numel_in()
(2, 9, 11)


>> f = Function('f', {SX.sym('x', 2), SX.sym('D', Sparsity.diag(3))}, {SX(1)});
>> fprintf('%d %d %d\n', f.nnz_in(0), f.nnz_in(1), f.nnz_in())
2 3 5
>> fprintf('%d %d %d\n', f.numel_in(0), f.numel_in(1), f.numel_in())
2 9 11


julia> D = ca.SX.sym("D", ca.Sparsity.diag(3));
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [ca.SX.sym("x", 2), D], [ca.SX(1)]);
julia> ca.nnz_in(f, 0), ca.nnz_in(f, 1), ca.nnz_in(f)
(2, 3, 5)
julia> ca.numel_in(f, 0), ca.numel_in(f, 1), ca.numel_in(f)
(2, 9, 11)

See also
nnz_out, numel_in, sparsity_in

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1vc

◆ nominal_in()

◆ nz_from_in()

std::vector<double> casadi::Function::nz_from_in ( const std::vector< DM > &  arg) const

nonzeros

nz_from_in flattens a list of n_in() DM arguments (shape rules as in call) into the concatenated nonzeros, in input order and column-major within each input; nz_to_in does the reverse, the list must have exactly nnz_in() entries. nz_from_out and nz_to_out do the same for the outputs with nnz_out(). This is the memory layout that generated C code and FunctionBuffer use.

>>> f = Function("f", [SX.sym("x"), SX.sym("D", Sparsity.diag(2))], [SX(0)])
>>> f.nz_from_in([1, DM([[2, 0], [0, 3]])])
[1.0, 2.0, 3.0]
>>> f.nz_to_in([1, 2, 3])
[DM(1), DM(
[[2, 00],
 [00, 3]])]
>>> try:
...   f.nz_to_in([1, 2])
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("Dimension mismatch. Expecting 3, got 2" in str(e))
True


>> f = Function('f', {SX.sym('x'), SX.sym('D', Sparsity.diag(2))}, {SX(0)});
>> f.nz_from_in({1, DM([2, 0; 0, 3])})
1 2 3
>> arg = f.nz_to_in([1, 2, 3]); disp(arg{1}), disp(arg{2})
1
[[2, 00],
 [00, 3]]
>> try, f.nz_to_in([1, 2]); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'Expecting 3, got 2')); end
1


julia> D = ca.SX.sym("D", ca.Sparsity.diag(2));
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [ca.SX.sym("x"), D], [ca.SX(0)]);
julia> ca.nz_from_in(f, [1, ca.DM([2 0; 0 3])])
3-element Vector{Float64}:
 1.0
 2.0
 3.0
julia> x_in, D_in = ca.nz_to_in(f, [1, 2, 3]);
julia> x_in
DM(1)
julia> D_in
DM(
[[2, 00],
 [00, 3]])
julia> try
           ca.nz_to_in(f, [1, 2])
       catch e
           println(occursin("Dimension mismatch. Expecting 3, got 2", sprint(showerror, e)))
       end
true

◆ oracle()

Function casadi::Function::oracle ( ) const

For nlpsol, qpsol, rootfinder and integrator objects this is the Function with the problem expressions (e.g. nlp:(x,p)->(f,g)); for other classes an error is raised.

>>> x = SX.sym("x")
>>> opts = {"qpsol": "qrqp", "print_header": False, "print_iteration": False,
...         "print_time": False, "qpsol_options": {"print_header": False}}
>>> S = nlpsol("S", "sqpmethod", {"x": x, "f": x**2}, opts)
>>> S.oracle()
Function(nlp:(x,p[])->(f,g[0]) SXFunction)
>>> try:
...   S.oracle().oracle()
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("'oracle' not defined for SXFunction" in str(e))
True


>> x = SX.sym('x');
>> opts = struct('qpsol', 'qrqp', 'print_header', false, 'print_iteration', false);
>> opts.print_time = false; opts.qpsol_options = struct('print_header', false);
>> S = nlpsol('S', 'sqpmethod', struct('x', x, 'f', x^2), opts);
>> S.oracle()
nlp:(x,p[])->(f,g[0]) SXFunction
>> try, S.oracle().oracle(); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'not defined')); end
1


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x");
julia> opts = Dict("qpsol" => "qrqp", "print_header" => false, "print_iteration" => false,
                   "print_time" => false, "qpsol_options" => Dict("print_header" => false));
julia> S = ca.nlpsol("S", "sqpmethod", Dict("x" => x, "f" => x^2), opts);
julia> ca.oracle(S)
Function(nlp:(x,p[])->(f,g[0]) SXFunction)
julia> try
           ca.oracle(ca.oracle(S))
       catch e
           println(occursin("'oracle' not defined for SXFunction", sprint(showerror, e)))
       end
true

See also
get_function, find_functions

◆ reverse()

Function casadi::Function::reverse ( casadi_int  nadj) const

The result is named adj<nadj>_<name> and has n_in + n_out + n_out inputs: the original inputs, the original outputs (out_<name>) and the adjoint seeds adj_<output>, each with nadj times the columns of the output. Its n_in outputs adj_<input> are the adjoint sensitivities, nadj stacked horizontally. Falls back to the full Jacobian when the class has no reverse mode; raises when it has no derivatives at all. Results are cached while alive. nadj must be positive.

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); y = SX.sym("y", 2)
>>> f = Function("f", [x, y], [x*y, sin(x)], ["x", "y"], ["r", "s"])
>>> R = f.reverse(1)
>>> R
Function(adj1_f:(x,y[2],out_r[2x1,0nz],out_s[1x1,0nz],adj_r[2],adj_s)->(adj_x,adj_y[2])
SXFunction)
>>> R(1, [2, 3], 0, 0, [1, 1], 0)
(DM(5), DM([1, 1]))


>> x = SX.sym('x'); y = SX.sym('y', 2);
>> f = Function('f', {x, y}, {x*y, sin(x)}, {'x', 'y'}, {'r', 's'});
>> R = f.reverse(1); disp(R)
adj1_f:(x,y[2],out_r[2x1,0nz],out_s[1x1,0nz],adj_r[2],adj_s)->(adj_x,adj_y[2]) SXFunction
>> [adj_x, adj_y] = R(1, [2; 3], 0, 0, [1; 1], 0); disp(adj_x), disp(adj_y)
5
[1, 1]


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x"); y = ca.SX.sym("y", 2);
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x, y], [x*y, sin(x)], ["x", "y"], ["r", "s"]);
julia> R = f.reverse(1);
julia> R
Function(adj1_f:(x,y[2],out_r[2x1,0nz],out_s[1x1,0nz],adj_r[2],adj_s)->(adj_x,adj_y[2]) SXFunction)
julia> R(1, [2, 3], 0, 0, [1, 1], 0)
(5, [1, 1])

See also
forward, jacobian, factory

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1wr

◆ save()

void casadi::Function::save ( const std::string &  fname,
const Dict &  opts = Dict() 
) const

Writes a serialization of the function to fname (conventionally with the extension .casadi). The file is portable across platforms and languages (Python, MATLAB, C++); it is compatible across CasADi versions as far as the serialization format allows.

Parameters
fnameFile name
optsSerializer options, e.g. "debug"

>>> import os
>>> x = SX.sym("x")
>>> f = Function("f", [x], [x**2])
>>> f.save("f.casadi")
>>> g = Function.load("f.casadi")
>>> g, g(3)
(Function(f:(i0)->(o0) SXFunction), DM(9))
>>> os.remove("f.casadi")


>> x = SX.sym('x');
>> f = Function('f', {x}, {x^2});
>> f.save('f.casadi'); g = Function.load('f.casadi'); disp(g), disp(g(3))
f:(i0)->(o0) SXFunction
9
>> delete('f.casadi');


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x");
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x], [x^2]);
julia> ca.save(f, "f.casadi")
julia> g = ca.Function.load("f.casadi");
julia> println(g); g(3)
f:(i0)->(o0) SXFunction
DM(9)
julia> rm("f.casadi")

See also
load, serialize, FileSerializer

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_240

◆ serialize()

std::string casadi::Function::serialize ( const Dict &  opts = Dict()) const

Any Function can be serialized, including solvers and functions with embedded calls; a just-in-time compiled function is stored according to option "jit_serialize". The string is binary-safe text; it is also what the Python pickle protocol and MATLAB save use.

Parameters
opts"debug" (bool) adds consistency markers to the stream

>>> x = SX.sym("x")
>>> f = Function("f", [x], [sin(x)], ["x"], ["y"])
>>> s = f.serialize()
>>> g = Function.deserialize(s)
>>> g, g(1)
(Function(f:(x)->(y) SXFunction), DM(0.841471))


>> x = SX.sym('x');
>> f = Function('f', {x}, {sin(x)}, {'x'}, {'y'});
>> s = f.serialize(); g = Function.deserialize(s); disp(g), disp(g(1))
f:(x)->(y) SXFunction
0.841471


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x");
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x], [sin(x)], ["x"], ["y"]);
julia> s = ca.serialize(f);
julia> g = ca.Function.deserialize(s);
julia> println(g); g(1)
f:(x)->(y) SXFunction
DM(0.841471)

See also
deserialize, save, load

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1x2

◆ size1_in() [1/2]

casadi_int casadi::Function::size1_in ( casadi_int  ind) const

size1_in is the number of rows, size2_in the number of columns and size_in the pair (rows, columns). The input is selected by index or by name; an unknown name raises an error listing the available names, an index out of range raises an error.

>>> f = Function("f", [SX.sym("A", 2, 3), SX.sym("b", 2)], [SX(0)], ["A", "b"], ["c"])
>>> f.size_in(0), f.size1_in("A"), f.size2_in("A")
((2, 3), 2, 3)
>>> f.size_in("b")
(2, 1)
>>> try:
...   f.size_in("nope")
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("Available names are: [A, b]" in str(e))
True


>> f = Function('f', {SX.sym('A', 2, 3), SX.sym('b', 2)}, {SX(0)}, {'A', 'b'}, {'c'});
>> disp(f.size_in(0)), fprintf('%d %d\n', f.size1_in('A'), f.size2_in('A'))
2 3
2 3
>> f.size_in('b')
2 1
>> try, f.size_in('nope'); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'Available names')); end
1


julia> A = ca.SX.sym("A", 2, 3); b = ca.SX.sym("b", 2);
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [A, b], [ca.SX(0)], ["A", "b"], ["c"]);
julia> ca.size_in(f, 0), ca.size1_in(f, "A"), ca.size2_in(f, "A")
((2, 3), 2, 3)
julia> ca.size_in(f, "b")
(2, 1)
julia> try
           ca.size_in(f, "nope")
       catch e
           println(occursin("Available names are: [A, b]", sprint(showerror, e)))
       end
true

◆ size1_out() [1/2]

casadi_int casadi::Function::size1_out ( casadi_int  ind) const

Same conventions as size_in, size1_in and size2_in.

>>> x = SX.sym("x", 3)
>>> f = Function("f", [x], [x.T, SX(2, 2)])

((1, 3), (2, 2), 1, 2)

1 3
2 2
>> fprintf('%d %d\n', f.size1_out(0), f.size2_out(1))
1 2

◆ size2_in() [1/2]

casadi_int casadi::Function::size2_in ( casadi_int  ind) const

size1_in is the number of rows, size2_in the number of columns and size_in the pair (rows, columns). The input is selected by index or by name; an unknown name raises an error listing the available names, an index out of range raises an error.

>>> f = Function("f", [SX.sym("A", 2, 3), SX.sym("b", 2)], [SX(0)], ["A", "b"], ["c"])
>>> f.size_in(0), f.size1_in("A"), f.size2_in("A")
((2, 3), 2, 3)
>>> f.size_in("b")
(2, 1)
>>> try:
...   f.size_in("nope")
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("Available names are: [A, b]" in str(e))
True


>> f = Function('f', {SX.sym('A', 2, 3), SX.sym('b', 2)}, {SX(0)}, {'A', 'b'}, {'c'});
>> disp(f.size_in(0)), fprintf('%d %d\n', f.size1_in('A'), f.size2_in('A'))
2 3
2 3
>> f.size_in('b')
2 1
>> try, f.size_in('nope'); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'Available names')); end
1

((2, 3), 2, 3)

(2, 1)

       end

◆ slice()

Function casadi::Function::slice ( const std::string &  name,
const std::vector< casadi_int > &  order_in,
const std::vector< casadi_int > &  order_out,
const Dict &  opts = Dict() 
) const

The expressions are unchanged; only the lists of inputs and outputs are rearranged. Indices may be repeated or omitted. An omitted input that an output depends on makes the result have free variables (construction then fails unless "allow_free" is in opts). Classes other than SXFunction and MXFunction are wrapped first.

Parameters
nameName of the result
order_inIndices of the original inputs to keep, in the new order
order_outIndices of the original outputs to keep, in the new order
optsOptions for the new function

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); y = SX.sym("y")
>>> f = Function("f", [x, y], [x + y, x - y, 2*x], ["x", "y"], ["a", "b", "c"])
>>> g = f.slice("g", [1, 0], [2, 0])
>>> g, g(1, 2)
(Function(g:(y,x)->(c,a) SXFunction), (DM(4), DM(3)))
>>> f.slice("h", [0], [2])
Function(h:(x)->(c) SXFunction)


>> x = SX.sym('x'); y = SX.sym('y');
>> f = Function('f', {x, y}, {x + y, x - y, 2*x}, {'x', 'y'}, {'a', 'b', 'c'});
>> g = f.slice('g', [1, 0], [2, 0]); disp(g)
g:(y,x)->(c,a) SXFunction
>> [c, a] = g(1, 2); disp(c), disp(a)
4
3
>> f.slice('h', 0, 2)
h:(x)->(c) SXFunction


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x"); y = ca.SX.sym("y");
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x, y], [x + y, x - y, 2x], ["x", "y"], ["a", "b", "c"]);
julia> g = ca.slice(f, "g", [1, 0], [2, 0]);
julia> println(g); g(1, 2)
g:(y,x)->(c,a) SXFunction
(4, 3)
julia> ca.slice(f, "h", [0], [2])
Function(h:(x)->(c) SXFunction)

See also
factory

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1wl

◆ sparsity_in() [1/2]

const Sparsity& casadi::Function::sparsity_in ( casadi_int  ind) const

Numerical arguments are projected onto this pattern before evaluation: entries outside it are dropped silently. Symbolic inputs keep the pattern of the expression passed to the constructor.

>>> f = Function("f", [SX.sym("D", Sparsity.diag(2))], [SX(0)], ["D"], ["r"])
>>> f.sparsity_in(0)
Sparsity(2x2,2nz)
>>> f.sparsity_in("D").is_diag()
True
>>> f.sparsity_out(0)
Sparsity(1x1)


>> f = Function('f', {SX.sym('D', Sparsity.diag(2))}, {SX(0)}, {'D'}, {'r'});
>> f.sparsity_in(0)
2x2,2nz
>> f.sparsity_in('D').is_diag()
1
>> f.sparsity_out(0)
1x1


julia> D = ca.SX.sym("D", ca.Sparsity.diag(2));
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [D], [ca.SX(0)], ["D"], ["r"]);
julia> ca.sparsity_in(f, 0)
Sparsity(2x2,2nz)
julia> ca.is_diag(ca.sparsity_in(f, "D"))
true
julia> ca.sparsity_out(f, 0)
Sparsity(1x1)

See also
sparsity_out, size_in, nnz_in

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1vr

◆ stats()

Dict casadi::Function::stats ( int  mem = 0) const

The content depends on the class: solvers report e.g. "success", "return_status", "iter_count"; with option "record_time" every function reports "t_wall_total", "t_proc_total" and "n_call_total". Raises an error when the function has not been evaluated numerically yet.

Parameters
memMemory object (from checkout) whose statistics to return; 0 is the memory used by ordinary calls

>>> x = SX.sym("x")
>>> f = Function("f", [x], [x**2], {"record_time": True})
>>> try:
...   f.stats()
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("first evaluate it numerically" in str(e))
True
>>> r = f(2)
>>> sorted(f.stats().keys())
['n_call_total', 't_proc_total', 't_wall_total']


>> x = SX.sym('x');
>> f = Function('f', {x}, {x^2}, struct('record_time', true));
>> try, f.stats(); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'first evaluate it numerically')); end
1
>> r = f(2); disp(strjoin(sort(fieldnames(f.stats()))', ' '))
n_call_total t_proc_total t_wall_total


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x");
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x], [x^2], Dict("record_time" => true));
julia> try
           ca.stats(f)
       catch e
           println(occursin("first evaluate it numerically", sprint(showerror, e)))
       end
true
julia> r = f(2);
julia> sort(collect(keys(ca.stats(f))))
3-element Vector{String}:
 "n_call_total"
 "t_proc_total"
 "t_wall_total"

See also
checkout

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_2mj

◆ sx_in() [1/3]

const std::vector<SX> casadi::Function::sx_in ( ) const

Convenient for calling the function symbolically or for building a wrapper: f(f.mx_in()) is an MX expression of the outputs. Each call creates new symbols; they are not the symbols the function was constructed from. All inputs (no argument), one by index, or one by name.

>>> f = Function("f", [SX.sym("a"), SX.sym("B", Sparsity.diag(2))], [SX(0)],
...              ["a", "B"], ["r"])
>>> f.sx_in()
[SX(a), SX(
[[B_0, 00],
 [00, B_1]])]
>>> f.mx_in(1), f.mx_in("a").shape
(MX(B), (1, 1))
>>> f.sx_out(), f.mx_out(0)
([SX(r)], MX(r))


>> a = SX.sym('a'); B = SX.sym('B', Sparsity.diag(2));
>> f = Function('f', {a, B}, {SX(0)}, {'a', 'B'}, {'r'});
>> in = f.sx_in(); disp(in{1}), disp(in{2})
a
[[B_0, 00],
 [00, B_1]]
>> f.mx_in(1), size(f.mx_in('a'))
B
1 1
>> out = f.sx_out(); disp(out{1}), disp(f.mx_out(0))
r
r


julia> a = ca.SX.sym("a"); B = ca.SX.sym("B", ca.Sparsity.diag(2));
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [a, B], [ca.SX(0)], ["a", "B"], ["r"]);
julia> a_in, B_in = ca.sx_in(f);
julia> a_in
SX(a)
julia> B_in
SX(
[[B_0, 00],
 [00, B_1]])
julia> ca.mx_in(f, 1), size(ca.mx_in(f, "a"))
(B, (1, 1))
julia> ca.sx_out(f), ca.mx_out(f, 0)
(SX[r], r)

◆ sx_in() [2/3]

const SX casadi::Function::sx_in ( casadi_int  iind) const

Convenient for calling the function symbolically or for building a wrapper: f(f.mx_in()) is an MX expression of the outputs. Each call creates new symbols; they are not the symbols the function was constructed from. All inputs (no argument), one by index, or one by name.

>>> f = Function("f", [SX.sym("a"), SX.sym("B", Sparsity.diag(2))], [SX(0)],
...              ["a", "B"], ["r"])
>>> f.sx_in()
[SX(a), SX(
[[B_0, 00],
 [00, B_1]])]
>>> f.mx_in(1), f.mx_in("a").shape
(MX(B), (1, 1))
>>> f.sx_out(), f.mx_out(0)
([SX(r)], MX(r))


>> a = SX.sym('a'); B = SX.sym('B', Sparsity.diag(2));
>> f = Function('f', {a, B}, {SX(0)}, {'a', 'B'}, {'r'});
>> in = f.sx_in(); disp(in{1}), disp(in{2})
a
[[B_0, 00],
 [00, B_1]]
>> f.mx_in(1), size(f.mx_in('a'))
B
1 1
>> out = f.sx_out(); disp(out{1}), disp(f.mx_out(0))
r
r


julia> a = ca.SX.sym("a"); B = ca.SX.sym("B", ca.Sparsity.diag(2));
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [a, B], [ca.SX(0)], ["a", "B"], ["r"]);
julia> a_in, B_in = ca.sx_in(f);
julia> a_in
SX(a)
julia> B_in
SX(
[[B_0, 00],
 [00, B_1]])

(B, (1, 1))
julia> ca.sx_out(f), ca.mx_out(f, 0)
(SX[r], r)

◆ transform() [1/4]

Function casadi::Function::transform ( const Dict &  opts = Dict()) const

Only defined for SXFunction and MXFunction. The result has the same signature, names and options; only the expression graph differs.

The dict form runs a single "simplify" pass whose tasks are selected with boolean options, all defaulting to on except combine_terms:

  • empty_inputs: an input whose symbols do not occur in any output is replaced by an all-zero sparse matrix of the same shape (its nnz becomes 0)
  • cse: common subexpression elimination
  • ref_count: drop unreferenced nodes, repeated until a fixed point
  • const_folding: evaluate constant subexpressions
  • combine_terms: merge like terms of sums

Unknown keys raise an error.

The passes form runs an explicit list of passes in order. Each pass is a list whose first entry is the verb:

  • ["simplify", task, ...] with tasks from the list above; an integer before a task is its run count (N>0: N times, 0: until the graph stops shrinking)
  • ["expand"]: expand to an SXFunction
  • ["external", library, operation] or [..., opts]: a transform from a plugin library

Here opts accepts only "verbose"; passing the boolean task options raises an error. With "verbose": true the node and instruction counts after each pass are printed.

Parameters
fnameName of the result; defaults to the current name
passesExplicit list of passes
optsDict form: task booleans and "verbose"; passes form: "verbose" only

>>> z = MX.sym("z"); w = MX.sym("w")
>>> f = Function("f", [z, w], [sin(z)*cos(z) + sin(z)*cos(z)], ["z", "w"], ["r"])
>>> f, f.n_instructions()
(Function(f:(z,w)->(r) MXFunction), 9)
>>> g = f.transform()
>>> g, g.n_instructions()
(Function(f:(z,w[1x1,0nz])->(r) MXFunction), 6)
>>> f.transform([["simplify", "cse"]]).n_instructions()
6
>>> f.transform("fe", [["expand"]])
Function(fe:(z,w)->(r) SXFunction)
>>> try:
...   f.transform([["nope"]])
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("unknown pass verb" in str(e))
True


>> z = MX.sym('z'); w = MX.sym('w');
>> f = Function('f', {z, w}, {sin(z)*cos(z) + sin(z)*cos(z)}, {'z', 'w'}, {'r'});
>> f, f.n_instructions()
f:(z,w)->(r) MXFunction
9
>> g = f.transform(); disp(g), disp(g.n_instructions())
f:(z,w[1x1,0nz])->(r) MXFunction
6
>> f.transform({{'simplify', 'cse'}}).n_instructions()
6
>> f.transform('fe', {{'expand'}})
fe:(z,w)->(r) SXFunction
>> try, f.transform({{'nope'}}); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'unknown pass')); end
1


julia> z = ca.MX.sym("z"); w = ca.MX.sym("w");
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [z, w], [sin(z)*cos(z) + sin(z)*cos(z)], ["z", "w"], ["r"]);
julia> println(f); ca.n_instructions(f)
f:(z,w)->(r) MXFunction
9
julia> g = ca.transform(f);
julia> println(g); ca.n_instructions(g)
f:(z,w[1x1,0nz])->(r) MXFunction
6
julia> ca.n_instructions(ca.transform(f, [["simplify", "cse"]]))
6
julia> ca.transform(f, "fe", [["expand"]])
Function(fe:(z,w)->(r) SXFunction)
julia> try
           ca.transform(f, [["nope"]])
       catch e
           println(occursin("unknown pass verb", sprint(showerror, e)))
       end
true

See also
expand

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_2ig

◆ uses_output()

bool casadi::Function::uses_output ( ) const

The functions returned by jacobian, forward and reverse always accept the original outputs as extra inputs (out_r, ...). For most classes those inputs are ignored and this returns false; it is true for classes that reuse the outputs, for example to avoid recomputing a solver solution.

>>> f = Function("f", [SX.sym("x")], [SX(0)])
>>> f.uses_output()
False


>> f = Function('f', {SX.sym('x')}, {SX(0)});
>> f.uses_output()
0


julia> f = ca.Function("f", [ca.SX.sym("x")], [ca.SX(0)]);
julia> ca.uses_output(f)
false

See also
jacobian, forward, reverse

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1w3

◆ which_depends()

std::vector<bool> casadi::Function::which_depends ( const std::string &  s_in,
const std::vector< std::string > &  s_out,
casadi_int  order = 1,
bool  tr = false 
) const

Returns one boolean per nonzero of input s_in: true when at least one of the outputs s_out depends on that entry (order 1) or depends on it nonlinearly (order 2). With tr true the relation is flipped: one boolean per nonzero of the vertically concatenated outputs, true when it depends on any entry of s_in. The analysis is structural (it follows the graph, not the values).

Parameters
s_inName of one input
s_outNames of the outputs to inspect
order1 (any dependence) or 2 (nonlinear dependence); other values raise
trTranspose the relation, see above

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); y = SX.sym("y", 2)
>>> f = Function("f", [x, y], [x*y[0], y[1]**2 + x, 3], ["x", "y"], ["a", "b", "c"])
>>> f.which_depends("y", ["a", "b"])
[True, True]
>>> f.which_depends("y", ["a", "b"], 2)
[False, True]
>>> f.which_depends("y", ["a", "b", "c"], 1, True)
[True, True, False]
>>> f.which_depends("x", ["a"], 2)
[False]


>> x = SX.sym('x'); y = SX.sym('y', 2);
>> f = Function('f', {x, y}, {x*y(1), y(2)^2 + x, 3}, {'x', 'y'}, {'a', 'b', 'c'});
>> f.which_depends('y', {'a', 'b'})
1 1
>> f.which_depends('y', {'a', 'b'}, 2)
0 1
>> f.which_depends('y', {'a', 'b', 'c'}, 1, true)
1 1 0
>> f.which_depends('x', {'a'}, 2)
0


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x"); y = ca.SX.sym("y", 2);
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x, y], [x*y[1], y[2]^2 + x, 3], ["x", "y"], ["a", "b", "c"]);
julia> ca.which_depends(f, "y", ["a", "b"])
2-element Vector{Bool}:
 1
 1
julia> ca.which_depends(f, "y", ["a", "b"], 2)
2-element Vector{Bool}:
 0
 1
julia> ca.which_depends(f, "y", ["a", "b", "c"], 1, true)
3-element Vector{Bool}:
 1
 1
 0
julia> ca.which_depends(f, "x", ["a"], 2)
1-element Vector{Bool}:
 0

See also
factory, jac_sparsity

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1vx

◆ wrap() [1/2]

Function casadi::Function::wrap ( ) const

The wrapper has the same signature and names, carries over the derivative options (ad_weight, ad_weight_sp, max_num_dir, is_diff_in/out, derivative_of) and is cached by name. Classes without their own derivative or slice implementation use it internally; it is also a way to give any function a plain MX graph.

Parameters
nameName of the wrapper; defaults to "wrap_" + name()

>>> x = SX.sym("x")
>>> f = Function("f", [x], [sin(x)], ["x"], ["y"])
>>> w = f.wrap()
>>> w, w.n_instructions(), w.get_function()
(Function(wrap_f:(x)->(y) MXFunction), 3, ['f'])
>>> f.wrap("g").name()
'g'


>> x = SX.sym('x');
>> f = Function('f', {x}, {sin(x)}, {'x'}, {'y'});
>> w = f.wrap(); disp(w), disp(w.n_instructions()), disp(w.get_function())
wrap_f:(x)->(y) MXFunction
3
f
>> f.wrap('g').name()
g


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x");
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x], [sin(x)], ["x"], ["y"]);
julia> w = ca.wrap(f);
julia> println(w); ca.n_instructions(w), ca.get_function(w)
wrap_f:(x)->(y) MXFunction
(3, ["f"])
julia> ca.name(ca.wrap(f, "g"))
"g"

See also
wrap_as_needed, expand

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1vv

◆ wrap_as_needed() [1/2]

Function casadi::Function::wrap_as_needed ( const Dict &  opts) const

Like wrap, but the wrapper is built with opts (merged with the derivative options of this function) and is not cached. The named overload returns this function itself when opts is empty and name equals its current name; the unnamed overload always wraps, under the name "wrap_" + name().

>>> x = SX.sym("x")
>>> f = Function("f", [x], [sin(x)])
>>> f.wrap_as_needed("f", {}).class_name(), f.wrap_as_needed({}).class_name()
('SXFunction', 'MXFunction')
>>> f.wrap_as_needed("g", {"ad_weight": 1}).name()
'g'


>> x = SX.sym('x');
>> f = Function('f', {x}, {sin(x)});
>> f.wrap_as_needed('f', struct()).class_name()
SXFunction
>> f.wrap_as_needed(struct()).class_name()
MXFunction
>> f.wrap_as_needed('g', struct('ad_weight', 1)).name()
g


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x");
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x], [sin(x)]);
julia> ca.class_name(ca.wrap_as_needed(f, "f", Dict()))
"SXFunction"
julia> ca.class_name(ca.wrap_as_needed(f, Dict()))
"MXFunction"
julia> ca.name(ca.wrap_as_needed(f, "g", Dict("ad_weight" => 1)))
"g"

See also
wrap

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1vw
